#5090. Why Hyperliquid fell over 8.03% in the last week despite listings on Upbit, Bithumb

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Hyperliquid [HYPE] has recently been stirring up chatter in the crypto space after reaching a three-month low.

The token struggled to attract any buying interest, especially after its recent listings on South Korea’s major exchanges, Upbit and Bithumb, ultimately falling 8.03% over the past week.

The recent pressure has been difficult for the token to escape, especially as the broader crypto market continues to struggle.

Notably, Bitcoin’s recent sell-off over the weekend that caused it to fall to $62,000, persistent ETF outflows, and widespread liquidations have kept investors feeling more cautious than ever.

As risk appetite has started to die down and selling pressure has shown little sign of easing, HYPE’s recovery in the near-term seems far-fetched.

At the time of writing, HYPE was trading at $66.88 on the weekly chart and had officially broken below the 23.6% Fibonacci level. This breach shifted attention to the token’s swing low at $60.045. A decline toward it would erase more of the token’s previous gains.

OBV had declined considerably from its September highs before flattening, indicating that buying pressure had failed to recover. Meanwhile, RSI stood at 48.03, slipping right below the neutral 50 mark, thus reflecting the token’s weakening momentum.

The daily chart offered further insight into the token’s activity and ultimately confirmed the bearish trend. Similar to the weekly chart, HYPE remained trading just below its 23.6% Fibonacci level at $68.78, with any attempt to reclaim this resistance proving to be an uphill battle.

OBV’s continued downtrend suggested that sellers have remained in control over the market. Additionally, the MACD remained below both its signal and zero lines, indicating bearish momentum is likely to persist, and the probability of a reversal occurring is highly unlikely.

As both timeframes pointed at continued downside risks, the $60.045 support level has become increasingly important for traders to pay attention to.

Losing this three-month low would trigger a deeper breakdown and expose HYPE to further losses, making any chances of a recovery a distant dream.

Ishika Guha is a Blockchain Technology Analyst and Sponsored Content Writer at AMBCrypto. She specializes in analyzing the real-world applications of blockchain technology and its potential for widespread adoption, while bringing a unique and critical perspective to the crypto space.

#5089. The Week Jensen Huang Fell: How a Spy Scandal Toppled Nvidia’s CEO

The Week Jensen Huang Fell: How a Spy Scandal Toppled Nvidia’s CEO

In the span of a few days, the most powerful executive in artificial intelligence went from untouchable to unemployed.

Jensen Huang, who built Nvidia from a graphics-card maker into the roughly $4 trillion engine of the AI boom, resigned Thursday after 33 years, felled not by a bad earnings report but by a scandal with the texture of a spy thriller.

Here’s how a defining week for AI, and by extension crypto, unfolded.

The crisis began with reports that Huang had grown close to Alice Huang (no relation), an executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed, one of Nvidia’s largest Southeast Asian customers, whom U.S. intelligence officials reportedly suspected of gathering information for Beijing.

For a company at the center of the U.S.-China chip fight and a looming debate over tighter export controls, the national-security overtones were impossible to ignore. Nvidia’s board opened an independent investigation into whether Huang had been the target of a foreign intelligence operation, though it said it had no reason to believe company secrets or national security were compromised.

On Nov. 8, Huang apologized for being “unfaithful” to his wife, acknowledging the relationship.

What might have been a single lapse quickly snowballed. The New York Times reported a second affair, with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia’s vice president of health care, who reported to Huang.

By Thursday, Huang was out as president, CEO and board member. Chief financial officer Colette Kress was named interim CEO, and longtime director Harvey Jones took over as board chair. The abruptness stunned an industry accustomed to treating Huang as synonymous with Nvidia itself.

Then, a day after his resignation, Reuters reported a third relationship, an “intimate” one with a second unnamed Nvidia employee, citing two people familiar with the matter. Reuters did not identify her, and Nvidia declined to comment. Huang has acknowledged only the Alice Huang affair, deflecting when the Times asked about Powell.

Nvidia’s stock cratered more than 14% at the open on Thursday before clawing back to close down about 4.6%. Wall Street quickly sorted winners from losers: rivals like AMD and Broadcom fared better, while companies tightly tied to Nvidia’s business took the brunt.

The reaction underscored how much of the AI trade, and the crypto market that increasingly moves with it, rests on a single company.

The scandal then rippled outward. Singapore barred Alice Huang from returning to the country, Alibaba shares slid on reports it was a Megaspeed customer, and congressional committees had already sought a closed-door briefing from Huang over his Megaspeed ties.

The loudest names in tech, AI and crypto called the ouster a blunder, with one founder branding it “a massive mistake.” Inside Nvidia, an internal suggestion-box post drew more than 1,600 comments, many pleading for Huang’s return. “If you believe in the mission, bring back THE boss,” one wrote.

The board has moved on regardless. According to the Financial Times, it approached Arm CEO Rene Haas, a former Nvidia executive who led Arm through its 2023 IPO, as a potential successor, though Nvidia called speculation about any candidate “premature.” Analysts have also floated Nvidia’s Ian Buck and AMD’s Lisa Su.

Nvidia’s chips power the AI data centers whose explosive buildout has become intertwined with crypto, from AI-agent commerce to the mining firms pivoting to host AI workloads.

Leadership turmoil at the company that supplies the picks and shovels ripples outward, and it lands amid a broader reckoning over AI’s role in everything from cyberattacks to data-center politics.

Nvidia reports earnings Nov. 17, with Kress at the helm and the permanent CEO search still underway, a nervous moment for a market already rattled by a sharp week-ending selloff.

For now, the company that defined the AI era is leaderless at the top, its founder’s legacy clouded by scandal, and the question hanging over Silicon Valley is whether anyone can fill the role Huang made uniquely his own.

#5088. Major Crypto-Stealing Exploit in JavaScript Emerges: Zoom, CNN, HSBC Affected

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Malicious JavaScript code snippets were planted on the websites of 178 organizations.

The exploit originated inside Optimizely's Web Experimentation platform, a tool that many large companies use for A/B testing, which means showing users multiple versions of a page to determine which one is more effective.

Report URI estimates that on November 16 the code sat in 188 of the 1,611 Optimizely snippets it crawled, and the real number is most likely higher.

Among the websites that could become victims of the hack are some of the largest names in the world: Zoom, the BBC, CNN, Fox News, Salesforce, GitLab, IKEA, HSBC, Equifax, Air France-KLM, Ledger and Peacock. BleepingComputer confirms that the script loads on many of them.

Essentially, the script's goal was to swap a wallet address on the page for the attacker's one, capture card numbers, take screenshots of pages and more. It was a whole stack of tools used to steal personal data, wallets and other assets.

According to Report URI, the attacker's wallets received about $7,040 within the first nine hours. That is a drop in the ocean considering the names involved, though the losses from card skimming are unknown. A card skimmer was added later that day, and a hidden iframe tried to load an iOS exploit chain on older iPhones.

Optimizely confirmed that an unauthorized party had exploited a vulnerability in Experimentation to add malicious JavaScript to certain customer projects. How the flaw worked is yet to be confirmed.

If successful, it could become one of the largest supply-chain attacks we have ever witnessed. Considering the attack came from a third party, we might see one of the largest service migrations in the world, as none of the third-party services and libraries can be trusted nowadays if used through an API.

Cryptocurrency services delegate certain tasks to third-party providers all the time, so witnessing a similar situation in our field is more than a possibility.

For product owners: stay cautious and rethink the use of services prone to exploits. For users: stay vigilant and try not to put all your eggs in one basket.

#5087. Binance Says Withdrawals Reopened on 87 Networks as Recovery Moves Toward Full Access

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Binance’s recovery has reached another practical milestone: the exchange says it reopened withdrawals across 87 networks during the week covered by its November 14 update. For customers waiting to move assets, that is more immediately useful than a general assurance about reserves.

The company notice targets reopening the remaining networks and assets by the end of the following week. It is a target for the restoration programme, rather than confirmation that every supported route is already operating.

The update names networks including Cardano, Avalanche, Stellar, Litecoin and NEAR. Binance asks customers to confirm the particular asset and network in its withdrawal interface, and warns that the rollout can involve processing delays.

The distinction between an asset and a network is operationally important. A token can be supported on more than one blockchain, while a customer’s receiving service may accept only a particular version. A broad announcement therefore cannot replace confirmation of the actual route at the time of transfer.

This extends the phased process described in NewsBTC’s report on the November 3 restart. The latest notice adds a larger disclosed set of restored networks and a target for the remaining work.

In a separate coverage announcement, Binance said it had completed replacement of affected assets after committing more than $6.34 billion of company resources. It described funding from SAFU, part of its futures insurance fund and its treasury.

The same disclosure said a November 12 reserve snapshot covered 198 tokens and that Binance intended to replenish SAFU to about $1 billion. These are company statements about funding and backing, with their own verification requirements.

They answer a different question from whether a customer can withdraw immediately. Restoring a balance, rebuilding an emergency reserve and enabling reliable transfers can progress at different speeds. Combining them into a single recovery percentage would hide the remaining operational work.

Binance’s proof-of-reserves explanation describes cryptographic checks that let customers confirm inclusion of their balances in a liabilities snapshot. Its system uses Merkle trees and zero-knowledge proofs to assess the relationship between included account data and the reported total.

That can provide useful evidence about the balances covered by a particular assessment. It does not, by itself, demonstrate that every security weakness has been removed or that every withdrawal request will process successfully.

NewsBTC recently examined the scope and limits of Bybit’s reserve reporting. The general lesson applies here too: readers need to know the date, assets and liabilities covered before deciding what a financial verification establishes.

A restoration programme becomes more convincing when published notices match the experience of customers using the affected services. That calls for clear lists of outstanding routes, updated explanations for delays and evidence that reopened services remain dependable.

The economic consequences can vary even among customers holding the same asset. Someone moving funds to a long-term wallet and a market maker relying on frequent transfers have different timing needs. Recovery quality includes predictable processing as well as the eventual return of a withdrawal button.

Binance has now supplied a concrete network count and a completion goal. The remaining question is whether the exchange can meet that goal and explain any exceptions clearly. Asset replacement and cryptographic reporting support that assessment, while actual access remains a separate result to observe.

#5086. Bitcoin’s 16% slide cost Yi He $97M, but her message to investors remains — HODL!

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Two developments have caught the market’s attention.

Yi He, Co-CEO of Binance, revealed that her portfolio lost a total of $97.32 million over the last 30 days (17 October – 16 November). After adding in all transfers, this amounted to a total loss of $101.31 million. This, after Bitcoin lost over 16% of its value over the same timeframe — Its largest monthly loss in over four months.

On the institutional front, JPMorgan has partnered with Singapore’s DBS Bank to launch a blockchain-based payment solution that allows institutional clients to transfer tokenized U.S dollar deposits between Singapore and the United States, 24/7.

Together, these developments show two different sides of the market.

On one hand, Yi He’s disclosure demonstrates just how deep the ongoing correction has been, with even major players taking a hit as selling pressure builds. On the other, JPMorgan’s partnership with DBS shows that institutional interest in blockchain is still holding up despite the FUD and broader market weakness.

This ties back to Yi He’s thesis that the ongoing pullback is only a short-term correction and there really isn’t any reason to panic. While prices continue to struggle, institutional activity suggests that long-term interest hasn’t disappeared.

Hence, the question is whether this conviction can eventually translate into a shift in sentiment.

According to the Bitcoin [BTC] Fear and Greed Index, sentiment levels fell to 10, indicating that the market was firmly in the “extreme fear” territory. The monthly average also seemed to be getting closer to this zone, with the same sitting at 28.2 at press time.

This suggests that fear is becoming more “persistent” rather than just a short-term reaction to market volatility. In fact, sentiment might soon be approaching levels last seen in February and June 2026, when Bitcoin faced deeper price declines.

And yet, Bitcoin’s Open Interest data showed that BTC was still accumulating new leveraged positions as it moved down from $83K to $74k. This may be evidence that while the market is becoming increasingly fearful, but it is yet to see leverage fully reset. This would leave BTC exposed to further volatility, especially if there isn’t fresh buying demand.

Against this backdrop, the significance of the two developments becomes clearer.

Extreme fear, Bitcoin’s 16% correction, and bearish on-chain signals could keep BTC under pressure in the near term. However, Yi He’s $97 million loss indicates not only the amount of stress on the market, but also that large institutions are continuing to increase/HODL their exposure to the blockchain space.

This might also mean that short-term market weakness has not necessarily changed the longer-term institutional outlook.

In this context, the 16% correction is more a general market correction rather than a loss of confidence in Bitcoin’s long-term potential.

Bitcoin’s 16% drop and high leverage point to further volatility.

JPMorgan’s partnership with DBS shows that blockchain adoption is on the right track, despite the downturn.

#5085. XRP Rich-List Sees Big Swing Trading Sub-$1

Home » Ripple (XRP) » XRP Rich-List Sees Big Swing Trading Sub-$1

Individuals ranking at the creme-de-la-creme spot of the XRP rich-list have upped the ante for newcomers.

Earning the top-tier wealth rankings on the XRP Ledger has become way easier since the native coin’s price dipped sub-$1. November’s XRP chain data registered 8.3 million funded accounts, highlighting a skewed distribution favoring smaller-type holdings, pushing the limits way lower.

The present XRP rich-list thresholds are as follows: 2,151 XRP for top 10%, 7,503 XRP for top 5%, 44,896 XRP for top 1%, and 275,420 XRP for top 0.1%, according to the latest blockchain explorer statistics. The entrance level got considerably lower, while the top 3% topped up more.

At the high-end of the XRP rich-list ranking, entering the top 0.01% requires an account balance of at least 3,836,116 XRP. The graphic estimates that approximately 804 accounts hold enough XRP to meet the threshold. Those figures represent accounts rather than unique individuals.

The $XRP Rich List has changed. 👀There are now more than 8 MILLION funded XRP accounts.To be in the:Top 10% → 2,151 XRPTop 5% → 7,503 XRPTop 1% → 44,896 XRPTop 0.1% → 275,420 XRPWhere do YOU rank? 🫵 pic.twitter.com/zaNgw91vc9

The snapshot of XRP’s account distribution matters due to the rotation of top whale holdings. While the broader crypto whales, otherwise known as large investors, have showcased multiple signs of distribution, the millionaire holders have vouched the possibility of buying the dip.

As reported by DailyCoin earlier this month, XRP hit a density high XRP’s in crypto millionaire concentration, the highest count since last December, implying monumental long-term price  conviction among the largest XRP HODLers. But the smaller players have acted differently.

The Chaikin Money Flow (CMF) has hit a stupendously low degree of -0.47, the lowest point since February 5, 2026. Back then XRP’s price was still trading above $1.51, but the sell-off spree had taken the veteran altcoin towards a local bottom of $1.05 in a brief moment of time.

Right now, XRP’s price stands at the mid-point territory of the 24-hour range. The OG altcoin’s trading at $0.953, bouncing off the lower intra-day channel boundary at $0.9414. Whipping up a rally above $1.10 will be crucial as the long versus short ratio keeps slightly bullish above 1.

#5084. NEAR Token Recovers to $3.26 as Governance Finalizes 1.6% Inflation Cut

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

House of Stake ratified HSP-029 on Nov. 16, and the proposal lowers the maximum annual issuance of NEAR Protocol from 2.5% to 1.6%. On Tuesday, the price of NEAR dipped to $3.12 in the morning and then went back up to $3.26.

HSP-029 was written by Sal Ternullo, CEO of SVRN, and opened for forum discussion on Oct. 7. The vote closed on Nov. 2, and 99.96% of the veNEAR cast was in favor of the proposal; no veNEAR was cast against it. The Security Council's 14-day window to veto the proposal closed on Nov. 16 with no veto, according to House of Stake.

This is the second inflation cut for NEAR in just over a year. In October 2025, the maximum issuance was halved from the original 5% to 2.5%, and in September 2026, the realized issuance stood at 2.45%, according to HSP-029.

At 1.6%, NEAR will have "one of the lowest annual issuance rates among major blockchains," House of Stake said.

According to House of Stake, the reduction will start one quarter after ratification, and validators still have to adopt the protocol upgrade. The issuance cap will reach 1.6% after 24 months.

Ternullo estimated in HSP-029 that NEAR will avoid issuing 11.8 million tokens over two years. At the current price, these tokens would be worth around $38.5 million.

The proposal also calculated that the staking yield of NEAR will go down from around 5.27% to around 3.37% if the staking ratio stays the same. More than half of all NEAR is not staked, according to House of Stake.

BitMEX co-founder Arthur Hayes recently named NEAR among four tokens that he expects to rise before the end of the AI bubble. Hayes set targets of $6 for NEAR, $2,800 for ETH, $1,800 for ZEC, and $40 for VVV.

"The size is small to medium as conviction is meh, but if the music is playing, you gotta dance," Hayes wrote on X.

The Four Horsemen shall pump and then usher in the end of the AI bubble: $ETH = $2800 $ZEC = $1800 $NEAR = $6.00 $VVV = $40 The size is small to medium as conviction is meh, but if the music playing you gotta dance.

Total volume through NEAR Intents, the cross-chain liquidity layer of the protocol, has reached $39 billion, and the service was recently integrated into Talos OEMS, an institutional trading platform.

#5083. Bitcoin ETFs Lose $2.1 Billion in November as Outflow Streak Hits Seven Days

Bitcoin ETFs Lose $2.1 Billion in November as Outflow Streak Hits Seven Days

Bitcoin ETF investors are still heading for the exits, and the price slide is speeding up.

U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs shed $185.4 million on Friday, according to Decrypt’s ETF tracker, extending their outflow streak to seven straight trading days.

The funds lost about $829 million this week after shedding nearly $1.3 billion the week before, bringing November’s outflows to roughly $2.1 billion.

The selling so far this month has been relentless. The funds bled $1.4 billion over five sessions earlier in the streak as investors cut risk. Cumulative net inflows since launch have slipped to $55.3 billion, and total net assets fell to $94.9 billion, below the $100 billion mark they held through much of October.

Other crypto funds haven’t escaped the rout. Ethereum ETFs took in a modest $2.3 million on Friday, snapping a six-day outflow streak, but still lost about $336 million on the week and roughly $702 million so far in November. Solana ETFs shed about $28 million this week, though they retain $1.6 billion in cumulative net inflows.

Spot crypto ETFs hold the underlying asset, such as Bitcoin or Ethereum, and trade on stock exchanges like ordinary shares, giving investors exposure through a regular brokerage account. Bitcoin funds have drawn $55.3 billion in net inflows since debuting in January 2024. Because issuers report flows daily and much of the money comes from institutions and financial advisers, the figures have become a closely watched gauge of investor sentiment.

The outflows have tracked a sharp sell-off. Bitcoin traded near $64,500 over the weekend, down 6% over 24 hours and about 15% on the week, according to CoinGecko, and it has lost more than 22% over the past month.

Ethereum fell 8.4% to about $1,790, and Solana dropped 12% to roughly $70. Total crypto market value slid 4.5% to about $2.3 trillion.

Bitcoin hit a 12-week low this week as fears of further Federal Reserve rate hikes erased its summer rally, and concentrated liquidations pushed the price below $69,000 earlier in the week.

Not every institution is retreating. JPMorgan’s holdings of Bitcoin and crypto-linked securities are approaching $1 billion, according to its most recent SEC filing. However, those purchases predate the more recent market downturn.

#5082. Wells Fargo Reports $2.35 Billion in BlackRock Bitcoin ETF Shares, but the Filing Needs Context

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

A new securities disclosure puts Wells Fargo among the largest reported holders of BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust, with a position valued at approximately $2.35 billion at the end of September. The scale is striking, but the story is not as simple as a bank buying billions of dollars of Bitcoin for itself. Form 13F reporting can include assets managed for customers, and it captures an older quarter-end rather than today’s trading. That distinction matters after Bitcoin’s sharp decline in November.

According to CoinDesk’s analysis of the filing disclosed on November 16, Wells Fargo reported about 49.58 million IBIT shares at September 30, compared with 24.4 million at the end of June. The share count therefore more than doubled. The value rose from roughly $812 million to $2.35 billion, a bigger percentage increase because the ETF also appreciated over the quarter. Reported shares and dollar valuations tell related but different stories: one indicates a change in positions, while the other incorporates market moves. A filing does not disclose each trade date, and it cannot identify which accounts made the purchases without additional information.

Wells Fargo’s investment-management reporting perimeter encompasses positions that may be held or managed for clients. It is not proof that the bank committed the entire reported amount from its own treasury. The 13F also concerns securities rather than Bitcoin held in an on-chain wallet. BlackRock’s fund holds the underlying asset; an investor in IBIT owns fund shares and gains exposure through their price. That structure is particularly important when comparing institutional reports. A large line item can reflect adviser activity, custody or delegated investment decisions rather than one central corporate allocation.

For related context, see our earlier report on JPMorgan's third-quarter bitcoin-fund disclosure.

The reported IBIT holding represents exposure to roughly 28,000 Bitcoin when scaled using the fund’s published quarter-end holdings and shares outstanding, according to the same analysis. That number should not be described as Bitcoin directly controlled by Wells Fargo. Nor does it tell us anything about whether the positions were retained during November’s sell-off. The September snapshot predates recent market weakness by weeks. Treating a delayed filing as evidence of current buying risks misreading both the timing and the investor behind the position.

There is nevertheless a useful trend here: the channels through which conventional financial institutions and their customers obtain Bitcoin exposure are expanding beyond dedicated crypto businesses. ETF shares can sit inside familiar investment operations, making reported positions easier to compare with conventional securities. The harder question is how persistent that exposure will prove when prices are falling and fund flows are negative. Fresh filings, fund creation and redemption data, and any changes in risk limits will offer a better indication than this one quarter-end snapshot.

The timing also matters for interpreting the filing. A third-quarter 13F arrives weeks after the positions it describes, and the September quarter-end share count cannot answer whether a manager bought or sold during the November drawdown. A client portfolio may be rebalanced for reasons unrelated to an institution's corporate conviction. For meaningful comparisons, analysts should check a consistent set of filing entities across quarters, rather than adding together affiliated accounts or equating changes in reported value with net purchases. That approach limits double counting and headline inflation.

For related context, see our earlier report on Harvard and Dartmouth's ETF filings.

The next disclosures will be more informative if they show how many shares remained in these reporting accounts at December 31. Until then, the figures support a statement about a large reported ETF position at September’s close, not a prediction about the next Bitcoin price move or the bank’s proprietary trading decisions.

Reporting sources: CoinDesk analysis of the newly disclosed 13F

Sui [SUI] recently reported that over the last 30 days, the stablecoin market cap on the Sui Network expanded by 10% to $538.35 million. In comparison, the total crypto stablecoin market cap fell by around 5% to around $284.9 billion at press time.

USD Coin drove the majority of this growth, according to DeFiLlama. In fact, gasless transfers on the network lowered costs and drew liquidity and DeFi activity to the ecosystem.

This is not enough reason to anticipate a SUI token price bounce by itself though. Especially since the wider crypto market has suffered heavy selling pressure in recent weeks.

AMBCrypto reported that the $0.827-level was an important support level and was being tested a few days ago. It was predicted that a lack of reaction at this level would mean further weakness for the token.

This came true as SUI’s reaction from around $0.82 was weak. After closing a daily session below this support level, since 12th November, the altcoin has shed 10.6% of its value in four days.

The weekly chart showed that SUI already had a bearish swing structure. The bounce to $1.25 in September was part of a retracement.

There have been seven straight red weekly candles since that rally. A drop below $0.65, the June swing low, may be very likely.

Last week, the $0.82-area was swept and a bounce appeared to be underway on Monday, 9th November. The price bounce proved to be muted.

A build-up of short liquidations just under $0.90 went untapped as selling pressure intensified.

The $0.795 and $0.745 pockets, where derivatives traders bet a price bounce would commence from, were also breached. The short-term price outlook was firmly bearish.

In the coming days, the $0.74-$0.76 area is expected to be a key supply zone to watch for traders to sell from. A move beyond $0.829 is needed to flip the short-term bearish bias.

#5080. Crypto Wallet Ledger Confirms Security Incident Involving Third-Party Service

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ledger, a crypto wallet firm, said it had experienced a security incident related to Optimizely, a third-party service used on its official website, but reassured its users about the security of their devices and private keys.

In a post, Ledger said it was aware of a security incident impacting Optimizely, a third-party service provider used by many other sites as well.

We are aware of a security incident affecting Optimizely, a third-party service used on https://t.co/d2pWxdG49X, alongside numerous other websites. Our team has remediated the issue, and the malicious code is no longer being served through our website. This incident does not affect the security architecture of Ledger devices. Your private keys remain protected by your Ledger device. For more information from Optimizely: https://t.co/CxzmBxYadb

Website optimization platform Optimizely said it had discovered and contained a security incident affecting some of its Web Experimentation customers on November 16, 2026. Optimizely stated that the vulnerable path has now been blocked, the injected code has been removed, and customers have been contacted directly.

Along these lines, Ledger confirmed that its team had remediated the issue and that the malicious code was no longer being served through its website.

Ledger emphasizes that the incident did not affect the security architecture of Ledger devices and that user private keys remain protected by their Ledger device, thus reassuring users that the incident had not compromised the core security of its wallet products.

According to researcher Scott Helme, on November 16, 2026, malicious JavaScript was served to visitors of 178 organizations' websites across several sectors through the Optimizely Web Experimentation platform. The websites included Zoom, the BBC, CNN, Salesforce, GitLab, IKEA, HSBC, Equifax, Air France-KLM, Ledger, and Peacock.

Most of the malicious code has now been removed, with only 1 of the 188 infected snippets found, down from 169.

The main point is that trusted JavaScript is not always trustworthy. A third-party dependency can exploit a website without ever breaking the website's own code or infrastructure.

#5079. Evernorth’s XRP Treasury Carries $505 Million of Write-Downs Under an Unusual Accounting Rule

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Evernorth’s first quarterly disclosure since its Nasdaq listing contains a number that demands an explanation: roughly $505 million in cumulative write-downs against XRP originally recorded at about $847 million. This is not the same as announcing that the company sold half a billion dollars of tokens at a loss. The Ripple-backed treasury business is applying an impairment model to certain holdings, and the resulting book value can sit well below their quoted market value. It is a lesson in how crypto company accounts can diverge from the assets investors think they are buying.

Evernorth reported around 346 million XRP with a carrying amount of $341.9 million at September 30. The filing shows approximately $233.7 million in impairment during 2025 and $271.0 million over the first nine months of 2026, adding to about $504.7 million. Those are noncash accounting reductions to the original recorded cost basis for the affected assets. The company’s filing also uses a September 30 Coinbase closing XRP price of about $1.49 elsewhere in its fair-value calculations. A market-price multiplication would therefore produce a different number from the historical carrying amount, illustrating why the two measures cannot be substituted for each other.

Many corporate crypto holdings now qualify for fair-value treatment that recognizes both price increases and decreases in earnings. Evernorth says its accounting relationship with Ripple means the XRP at issue does not qualify for that newer treatment. Instead, after a decline triggers an impairment, a subsequent recovery does not automatically lift the asset’s carrying value. The discrepancy is therefore partly structural. Two publicly listed firms can hold volatile digital assets while reporting dramatically different earnings patterns because the relevant accounting tests and related-party facts differ.

For related context, see our earlier report on another digital-asset treasury accounting example.

Investors should separate at least three ideas: cash spent to acquire XRP, the amount at which those tokens are currently recorded, and the market value of tokens still held. An impairment is not evidence of a wallet transfer or disposal. Equally, it should not be waved away as meaningless, because the rules shape reported net assets and future recognized gains. Evernorth also reported a roughly $33 million loss for the first nine months of 2026, supported by a substantial noncash gain elsewhere. Reading only the headline profit or loss would conceal much of that composition.

The real questions concern how the treasury is funded, whether its holdings change, what related-party restrictions remain relevant and how the accounting policy affects comparisons with other crypto-treasury companies. Investors should also examine issuance and dilution, not just the number of tokens acquired. Further quarterly reports may provide better evidence about deployment and operating results. Any claim that the write-down itself predicts XRP’s next move goes beyond what the September balance sheet can establish.

There is a further distinction between a company's token cost basis and the quoted market value of its holdings. Accounting classifications can produce different paths for recognising losses and subsequent gains, even when two firms own the same asset. Readers should look beyond the aggregated balance-sheet figure to the footnotes describing the impairment calculation, disposal assumptions and whether valuation recovery is permitted. Those policies may affect future reported earnings independently of XRP trading volumes, treasury spending or the company's commercial activity.

For related context, see our earlier report on financial-institution crypto disclosures.

The filing is most useful when read as a reconciliation between original cost, impairment and remaining token inventory. It explains the bookkeeping behind a dramatic headline without removing the economic risk of holding XRP. Shareholders still bear exposure to the token’s price, the company’s capital structure and its execution.

Reporting sources: Evernorth filing, SEC · CoinDesk report

#5078. Gate Adds GBP and EUR Banking Support for Gate Money via OpenPayd

Home » Exchanges » Gate Adds GBP and EUR Banking Support for Gate Money via OpenPayd

The expanded integration adds virtual pound accounts and euro SEPA rails across Gate’s unified financial platform.

Cryptocurrency and financial services platform Gate has expanded its multi-year partnership with financial infrastructure provider OpenPayd to support the international rollout of Gate Money, its unified fiat, crypto, and equities account.

Under the expanded agreement, Gate is integrating OpenPayd’s British pound (GBP) account infrastructure and euro (EUR) Single Euro Payments Area (SEPA) payment rails across additional corporate entities.

The expansion builds on a partnership established in 2022, when Gate began using OpenPayd’s virtual IBANs to manage and reconcile European retail and corporate payment flows.

According to the companies, the additional GBP and EUR capabilities will support Gate Money while remaining available across Gate’s broader platform, which serves more than 62 million users worldwide.

The infrastructure expansion follows the October 7, 2026 launch of Gate Money. The product brings digital assets, equities, payments and asset management together within a single account.

Users can hold, receive, transfer, pay and exchange GBP and EUR alongside digital assets, consolidating multiple financial activities within one platform.

The launch represents Gate’s expansion beyond cryptocurrency trading into broader financial services, with fiat payment infrastructure playing a central role in connecting traditional currencies and digital assets.

“Bringing assets, accounts and payments together requires fiat infrastructure that can operate across products and markets,” OpenPayd Chief Commercial Officer Lux Thiagarajah said in a statement.

Gate Founder and CEO Dr. Han said the expanded partnership provides the financial infrastructure needed to integrate Gate’s services and simplify everyday fund management and payments for its global user base.

Gate’s expansion reflects a broader push by crypto exchanges to become all-in-one financial platforms.

Coinbase opened stock and ETF trading to all US users in February, while Kraken and Bybit have expanded into tokenized equities.

Gate announced a crypto-linked Visa card in early October, that is expected to reach more than 40 markets, allowing users to spend their Gate Money balances at merchants. Its broader platform also provides access to metals, indices, forex and commodities, alongside more than 5,400 digital assets and 12,800 stocks and ETFs for trading.

For OpenPayd, the expanded agreement deepens its role in Gate’s financial services operations, strengthening its position as a provider connecting traditional banking with digital assets.

Founded in 2018, OpenPayd offers businesses a range of financial services through a single API, including embedded accounts, foreign exchange, domestic and international payments, Open Banking and stablecoin on- and off-ramps.

The company says it serves more than 1,200 businesses, including eToro, Kraken, OKX and B2C2, and processes over $360 billion in annual transaction volumes.

For London-based OpenPayd, the expanded mandate adds to a growing roster of digital asset clients—including Kraken, OKX, Bybit, eToro, and B2C2—that rely on its API for fiat on/off-ramps and stablecoin settlement.

Seamless fiat banking rails are the missing link for crypto exchanges trying to compete with traditional neobanks as everyday financial hubs. By wiring direct fiat infrastructure into their ecosystem, crypto platforms reduce reliance on third-party workarounds and make it easier for users to hold, convert, and spend traditional currencies alongside crypto and equities.

#5076. Bet25.com Launches Zero Margin Sportsbook to Eliminate Hidden House Edges

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Every sports bettor deals with the same issue: traditional sportsbooks hide extra profit margins within their odds. Behind flashy ads and complicated bonuses, regular bookmakers quietly lower payouts. When building a parlay or combo bet, these hidden cuts stack up fast.

Bet25 is changing this with the Zero Margin Sportsbook, setting the new rules of sports betting. Built on honesty, clear numbers, and fair pricing, Bet25 removes traditional bookmaker tricks, so the real payout is received.

In a standard sportsbook, the odds you see are not the true odds. The bookmaker trims 5% to 10% off the price of each game to guarantee their own cut before you even place a bet.

On a single match, this might look small. But on combo bets, it becomes a serious problem. Because combo odds multiply together, the hidden house cuts multiply too. Every leg you add gives you a payout that is much lower than the actual math says it should be.

The Bet25 Zero Margin Sportsbook is not a temporary promo or a short-lived boost. It is a completely different way to run a sportsbook:

A normal bookmaker takes a cut on pick 1, pick 2, pick 3, and so on. Bet25 charges one single 5% fee whether you pick one game or five.

The difference is easy to see on a multi-leg ticket. Take a simple 5-leg combo where each pick has a true 50/50 probability. A regular bookmaker will usually offer around 1.87 on each outcome to keep their cut, but a zero-margin sportsbook will price it at 2.0, again because of no hidden margin inside.

Same five picks. Same $100 bet. Exactly the same risk. But Bet25 pays out $755 more because no hidden cuts were multiplied along the way.

Almost every betting site claims to have “the best odds.” Bet25 doesn’t need hype or marketing talk. Instead, the advice is simple: just compare.

Open a betting site side by side with the Bet25 Zero Margin Sportsbook. Check any match, build a 3-leg or 4-leg combo on both, and look at the final payouts. You will see the difference right away. It is not marketing – it is pure math.

Turning a concept this disruptive into reality depended on having the right technological backbone. Bet25 teamed up with Vegangster as its primary platform partner, working side-by-side from initial ideation straight through to full deployment. Vegangster’s flexible, cutting-edge architecture gave the team the technical freedom to execute bold and complex features without friction.

In an industry where legacy tech often limits innovation, having a responsive partner who can translate ambitious mechanics into reality has been essential to Bet25’s launch.

Sports betting should be about making smart picks and getting paid fairly when you are right. By creating the new rules of sports betting, removing the hidden house edge, and offering naturally better odds, Bet25 gives players a clear, honest alternative to old-school sportsbooks.

Bet25.com operates under a gaming licence issued by the Government of the Autonomous Island of Anjouan, Union of the Comoros, under Licence No. ALSI-202505001-FI1.

#5075. Judge Acquits Brothers in $25 Million Ethereum MEV Case After Finding No Proven Wire Fraud

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

A Manhattan federal judge has acquitted Anton and James Peraire-Bueno of criminal charges stemming from a 2023 Ethereum trading maneuver that prosecutors said extracted about $25 million. The decision is important, but the takeaway needs precision. It does not amount to judicial approval of every aggressive trading technique on public blockchains. Instead, the judge found that prosecutors failed to prove the material misrepresentations necessary for wire fraud and related convictions in this particular case.

The case concerned maximal extractable value, or MEV, the ability to affect the ordering or inclusion of blockchain transactions for financial gain. Prosecutors said the brothers engineered a bait-and-switch involving Ethereum validator infrastructure and trading bots in April 2023. Their alleged manipulation diverted value that the bots otherwise expected to capture. The defense contested the fraud theory. The unusual technical setting made the case a test of how existing criminal statutes apply to blockchain transactions that may be opportunistic or adversarial but do not necessarily resemble ordinary deceptive financial communications.

US District Judge Jessica Clarke focused on whether the conduct, as charged and proved, met the legal requirements for fraud. According to the ruling reported by Reuters and The Block, the relevant transactions did not involve material false representations of the kind prosecutors needed to establish. The judge distinguished that legal test from the question of whether the behavior was fair, ethical or objectionable. She also noted the absence of a regulated-market rule prohibiting the charged conduct in that setting. An acquittal under particular charges does not pronounce on every possible theory of liability.

For related context, see our earlier report on the Kava Lend collateral exploit.

The brothers were arrested in May 2024, and a jury failed to reach a verdict in November 2025. That sequence matters when interpreting Monday’s ruling: the result followed substantial litigation rather than a quick dismissal immediately after an indictment. The court’s reasoning offers a specific limit on a criminal fraud case, not a complete set of rules for MEV markets. Other facts, jurisdictions or statutory claims could produce different outcomes. Describing the ruling as the legalization of MEV attacks would overstate what the court decided.

MEV remains a practical concern for users who trade on-chain. Transaction ordering, block building and the design of trading bots can affect execution prices and who captures value. Better routing, slippage controls and protocol-level improvements may reduce some forms of extraction, but they do not eliminate competition over transaction priority. Legal certainty matters for developers and traders, while consumer protection remains a separate operational issue. The industry will be watching how prosecutors and policymakers respond to the limits the judge identified.

A ruling on criminal liability also leaves open the technical question of how MEV infrastructure should be designed. Relays, builders and validators can change their software or operational policies, but those changes have different trade-offs for permissionlessness, censorship resistance and execution fairness. Developers can study the facts of this case without treating the acquittal as a blanket endorsement of every form of transaction manipulation. The legal finding is narrower: the government did not prove the fraudulent representation required for the charged offences on the trial record.

For related context, see our earlier report on the Clearpool token-migration safeguards.

The headline is an acquittal in a closely watched federal case, not a blanket safe harbour for exploiting counterparties. For Ethereum users, it reinforces the need to distinguish legal allegations, proven misrepresentations and the technical safeguards available during on-chain execution.

Reporting sources: Reuters reporting on the federal ruling · The Block court report

#5074. How Wall Street Is Racing to Claim the Onchain Dollar

How Wall Street Is Racing to Claim the Onchain Dollar

For years, stablecoins were crypto’s business, dominated by Tether and Circle and used mostly to trade digital assets. That era is ending.

Over the past few months, and especially the past few weeks, the biggest names in traditional finance have moved en masse to claim a piece of the onchain dollar. Here’s the map of a land grab that’s accelerating fast.

The clearest signal came this week, when Goldman Sachs, Citigroup, Santander, and Deutsche Bank were reported to be among banks committing roughly $270 million to a joint dollar-stablecoin venture codenamed “Project Faro.”

The capital funds five years of operations for a U.S.-incorporated issuer targeting a 2027 launch, part of a 21-institution coalition that has swelled from an initial group of 10. The goal is explicit: challenge Tether and Circle, which together still control about 85% of a roughly $290 billion market.

While the consortium plans, SoFi has shipped. Earlier this week, its SoFiUSD, billed as the first stablecoin from a U.S. national bank, expanded onto Polygon this month alongside Ethereum and Solana, reaching the bank’s 17 million-plus members.

SoFi also launched SoFi Earn, a tokenized deposit paying 4.40% yield that converts into SoFiUSD, and already settles its entire card program over Mastercard’s network.

Meanwhile, the federally chartered crypto bank Anchorage Digital has positioned itself as a settlement backbone.

Anchorage’s tokens can now settle transactions on Visa’s network, starting with Western Union’s USDPT, and it recently anchored both sides of the U.S.-Mexico stablecoin corridor, custodying tokens across one of the world’s largest remittance routes.

Mastercard has been the most aggressive incumbent among payment giants.

It launched a Global Money Platform with acquired stablecoin firm BVNK, rolled out a Stablecoin Security tool to screen payments for fraud, and unveiled Agent Pay for Machines, letting AI agents transact in stablecoins.

Visa, meanwhile, has expanded its own stablecoin settlement program across multiple blockchains.

The crypto-native incumbents, not to be left out, are also racing to entrench.

Circle struck a deal to bring USDC settlement to banking rails in Latin America, starting with a Colombia pilot, part of a broader expansion into dozens of emerging markets.

The catalyst is regulation. The GENIUS Act, signed in 2025, created the first U.S. framework for dollar-pegged tokens, giving regulated institutions a clear path into a business they had largely watched from the sidelines.

The Donald Trump administration has framed dollar stablecoins as a tool to extend the dollar’s reach and global hegemony, and banks that once dismissed crypto now see a settlement business they can’t afford to cede.

Much of this remains early. Project Faro has no token, no CEO, and no final name. Tether and Circle still dominate, and bank-issued tokens face a steep climb to win share.

But the direction is unmistakable: the onchain dollar, once crypto’s turf, is now contested ground, with Wall Street, the card networks and the stablecoin incumbents all racing to own the rails money moves on.

#5073. Bitcoin Holds $64K After US Bank Outages, but Demand Indicators Flash Warning

Home » Crypto Market News » Bitcoin Holds $64K After US Bank Outages, but Demand Indicators Flash Warning

Calm price action masks deeply negative on-chain demand and a shrinking profit cushion for Bitcoin holders.

Bitcoin price barely reacted to widespread online banking and payment delays across major U.S. banks on Monday, though on-chain data suggests the price stability may mask underlying weakness in demand.

Bitcoin (BTC) edged down 0.2% over the past 24 hours to trade at $64,183 on Tuesday. Crypto market liquidations totaled $278.3 million over the same period—a moderate figure pointing to an absence of forced selling or panic. Bitcoin short liquidations accounted for $88.67 million, according to CoinGlass.

The muted market reaction came despite significant disruptions in traditional US banking infrastructure. According to the Associated Press, a software bug in JPMorgan Chase’s internal systems slowed transaction processing on Monday and triggered ripple effects across the wider banking network.

Customers at several major institutions, including Wells Fargo, Bank of America, U.S. Bank, Truist, and Capital One, reported delays, particularly when sending or receiving peer-to-peer transfers via Zelle involving Chase accounts.

While traditional payment rails faced bottlenecks and renewed scrutiny over centralized infrastructure, traders largely treated the incident as a temporary operational hiccup rather than a catalyst to rotate into crypto.

Beneath the calm surface, however, on-chain metrics point to persistent buyer hesitation. A report by CryptoQuant analyst MorenoDV highlights that Bitcoin’s “apparent demand” remains deeply negative.

On November 15, the 30-day reading was around -247,700 BTC, while the one-year figure stood at approximately -1.21 million BTC, with Bitcoin trading near $65,000.

The analyst says the current contraction is among the sharpest negative readings in the period analyzed, with similar levels seen in March 2025 and mid-2026. However, he cautioned that the data does not mean Bitcoin is experiencing an unprecedented collapse or that its price has found a bottom.

What stands out more is how long the weakness has lasted. The 30-day reading has remained mostly negative throughout 2026, with only a brief recovery in late August.

The one-year reading also turned negative around late May and has continued to decline. In other words, short-term improvements in demand have not lasted long enough to signal a sustained recovery.

Bitcoin’s earlier recovery toward $80,000–$85,000 did little to improve the longer-term demand trend. According to MorenoDV, this suggests the rebound lacked strong underlying demand.

The analyst cautions that apparent demand is an indirect on-chain measure, not a direct record of BTC sales or capital outflows. Negative readings have also occurred during previous price recoveries, so the metric cannot reliably predict Bitcoin’s next move.

For a stronger recovery signal, the 30-day reading would need to improve consistently, return above zero and eventually stabilize on the annual measure. Until then, persistent weak demand remains the main concern.

Another on-chain indicator reinforces the cautious outlook. Analyst Axel Adler Jr. reported that the share of Bitcoin supply in profit fell sharply to 52% (down 7% in a week), leaving the market close to an even split between coins held at a profit and those held at a loss.

Meanwhile, long-term holder (LTH) supply fell by 73,900 BTC to 16.2 million BTC, its lowest level in 90 days.

According to Adler, both declines rank among the steepest of the past year, pointing to a shrinking profit cushion and faster redistribution of Bitcoin holdings. The market is now approaching a key threshold: below 50%, more than half of the circulating supply would be held at a loss.

A similar pattern emerged in August 2026, before Bitcoin rebounded by 24%. However, Adler cautions that one historical example is not enough to suggest the same recovery will happen again.

Bitcoin’s stability amid disruptions to traditional banking may suggest resilience, but the on-chain data tells a more cautious story. Persistent negative demand, a declining share of supply in profit and falling long-term holder balances point to weakening market support and a shrinking cushion against further price declines.

Although Shiba Inu has continued to struggle amid the persisting market volatility, showing substantial daily losses, the leading meme token has just reached a major milestone in its holder count.

#5070. Tokenized stocks are exactly where stablecoins were in 2020 – How?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Stablecoins started as a way to hold dollars on-chain. Then people started lending, trading, and earning with them. In time, DeFi grew up around them.

Tokenized stocks are heading down the same route now, and the resemblance is uncanny.

What’s worth noting here is how much of it is actually being used.

According to data from Token Terminal, tokenized stocks deposited into DeFi are now worth about $300 million. A year ago, that was at approx. $12.5 million.

This number didn’t move at all through late 2025. Things picked up in the spring, blasted through $100 million by around August, and then shot past a quarter million in September.

It’s also a bigger share of the market now. That $300 million is 6.3% of the $4.8 billion tokenized stock market cap. Last year, only 2.4% was being put to work.

Because once they’re there, holders can borrow against them, earn fees by providing liquidity, or chase yield.

And every new way to use them, is essentially one more reason not to cash out.

Tokenized stocks now have a market cap of about $4.8 billion. Stablecoins crossed that exact number in March 2020. Since then, stablecoins have grown, 59 times over! The number is at $285 billion now.

Tokenized stocks are moving fast too. They were worth only a few tens of millions in early 2025.

But why? A blockchain is basically a ledger for assets, and it gets more useful when it holds things people really want. U.S Stocks are about as in-demand as it gets.

Stablecoins didn’t get to where they are without the effort.

They made it through the COVID crash, the Terra collapse, FTX, and the USDC de-peg after SVB failed. Market cap fell by 28% between February 2022 and October 2023, then spiked up to a high by September 2024.

What kept them going though? Cash and T-bill reserves backed every token, plus people who actually wanted to use them.

Tokenized stocks follow the same 1:1 idea.

Except the risk factor is higher. So is the potential for gains.

The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. For the first time since the major drop, Shiba Inu has finally printed a substantial exchange outflow. According to data from CryptoQuant, more than 300 billion SHIB has been withdrawn from exchanges. It's a drop in the ocean, though: 312 billion SHIB is only around 0.36% of the 86 trillion tokens held on exchanges.

Shiba Inu has been struggling substantially on the market, invalidating one price level after another. The only positive development here is the descending trading volume, which reflects the possibility of a price recovery.

The outflow is most likely fueled by retail investors, as Shiba Inu's liquidity is thin compared with institutional appetite. Most of the holders are regular retail investors. Investors aren't buying en masse, but there is some accumulation, which could be tied to automated market making.

Shiba Inu mostly followed the general uptrend but tumbled quickly due to the broader reversal of the cryptocurrency market. With the RSI at the bottom, the next step should be either a sideways trend or a gradual recovery. The market is currently moving purely on speculative accumulation that was triggered by the massive price drop across multiple assets.

The market is weak and might get weaker, but the most selling pressure SHIB could face has already been injected.

#5068. Elon Musk Joins 160,000 Backing Petition to Bring Jensen Huang Back to Nvidia

Elon Musk Joins 160,000 Backing Petition to Bring Jensen Huang Back to Nvidia

Elon Musk has thrown his support behind a campaign to reinstate Jensen Huang as Nvidia's CEO, as the ousted chipmaker co-founder reportedly weighs a comeback just days after resigning.

"A petition is underway to reinstate Jensen and I'm signing it too," the Tesla and SpaceX CEO tweeted on Friday. Musk had previously said it would be "an honor" to hire Huang.

A petition is underway to reinstate Jensen and I’m signing it too

— Elon Musk (@elonmusk) November 13, 2026

The "Stand with Jensen" open letter calls on Nvidia's board to bring Huang back while an independent investigation establishes the facts. It has drawn more than 160,000 signatures, including Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney and Y Combinator CEO Garry Tan, CNBC reported, though the site notes that signatories' affiliations are self-reported and unverified. More than 7,500 signatories identified themselves as Nvidia employees, The Guardian reported, a figure it said was equivalent to 18% of the company's workforce.

The campaign follows internal calls from Nvidia staff for Huang's return.

Huang has discussed the possibility of returning with confidants, the Financial Times reported, citing multiple people familiar with the matter. One ally told the newspaper they were assembling a "war room" of Silicon Valley executives to lobby for his reinstatement and would seek support from the White House, adding that the effort had Huang's backing.

Even supporters acknowledged the obstacles. "Having an affair with a subordinate is a fireable offence," one person involved in the effort told the FT.

Two major Nvidia shareholders, Altimeter Capital's Brad Gerstner and Atreides Management's Gavin Baker, urged the board in an open letter on Friday to leave "all options on the table, including the potential return of Jensen Huang."

Huang resigned as Nvidia's president, CEO and board member on Thursday, capping a week of reports of affairs with Alice Huang, a former executive at Nvidia customer Megaspeed whom U.S. officials suspect of spying for China, and with an Nvidia executive who reported to him. Chief financial officer Colette Kress is serving as interim CEO.

The campaign faces resistance in Washington. Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said on Sunday that Nvidia should look outside the company for its next CEO. "They need a dramatic shake-up," he told Fox News.

Bessent said on Sunday Morning Futures that questions over Nvidia's compliance with U.S. export restrictions were the bigger issue, and that the company should be judged on whether it had "intentionally ignored the warning signs" of shell companies in Singapore. He said he wanted answers "in the next 10 days from the board and from the outside counsel." Huang also faces congressional inquiries into Nvidia's chip exports to China, as well as a proposed shareholder class action.

Nvidia's board has approached Arm CEO Rene Haas as a potential successor, the FT has reported. "We are in the process of selecting a search firm for the NVIDIA CEO role. We have not formally engaged any potential candidates," an Nvidia spokesperson told CNBC.

Analysts are divided on Huang's chances. "We see no chance Jensen comes back to Nvidia as CEO," Dan Ives of Yorkville Ives & Co. said in a note cited by CNBC, while Seaport Research Partners' Jay Goldberg said Huang could stage "a second act" if he gets through his legal troubles. Former White House communications director Anthony Scaramucci was blunter: "The FBI doesn't take petitions," he tweeted.

Nvidia reports quarterly earnings on Tuesday, the first under Kress.

#5067. Uniswap freezes $300,000 in stranded fees, halting automatic token burns

Uniswap proposes a new bridge and declining UNI payment threshold to release roughly $300,000 in stranded X Layer fees.

Uniswap’s proposed repair for fees stranded on the X Layer network would change both the bridge used to burn UNI and the payment traders make to claim the backlog.

Uniswap Labs disclosed on Nov. 6 that protocol fees had been accruing on X Layer but could not be released, leaving the corresponding UNI unburned. Implementation reviews on Nov. 13-14 describe a higher initial UNI payment designed to reduce the windfall available to the first trader who clears that backlog.

Uniswap’s fee system relies on outside traders, known as searchers, supplying UNI in exchange for selected fee assets held in a TokenJar, a contract that collects those assets.

The payment goes toward burning UNI, and the searcher keeps the recoverable asset value above its UNI payment and execution costs.

Converting accrued fees into a completed UNI burn requires a searcher to execute the exchange and the bridge to settle it. The searcher’s net surplus is a separate return, so a release does not require Uniswap itself to buy UNI on the open market at that moment.

X Layer's governance messages use the OP Stack bridge, while token transfers use Agglayer. According to Labs, the installed relayer followed the usual OP Stack token-bridging pattern, but X Layer’s version rejects ERC-20 transfers, including UNI.

A searcher receives fee assets and supplies UNI. X Layer’s disclosed bridge mismatch interrupted the second leg; the proposed repair changes the route and payment.

Uniswap Labs said on Nov. 6 that fees accrued, but the installed OP token route prevented releases and corresponding UNI burns.

The plan uses Agglayer for UNI transfers and a declining payment. These are proposed mechanics; successful execution and settlement remain unestablished.

Accrued fees, searcher surplus and a settled UNI burn measure different things.

The failure state is Labs’ November 6 disclosure, not an independently measured November 15 balance. Repair execution is unestablished. PR parameters remain proposed and differ from the forum’s timing. Diagram widths do not encode value.

Source-bound mechanism diagram, not a quantitative allocation. Solid and dashed connectors distinguish the disclosed stalled route from the proposed route. The proposed parameters are reported from PR 167; no price curve or clearing result is calculated.

The failure state is Labs’ November 6 disclosure, not an independently measured November 15 balance. Repair execution is unestablished. PR parameters remain proposed and differ from the forum’s timing. Diagram widths do not encode value.

Proposal 95 records execution on March 8, including X Layer fee-activation actions. The proposed replacement would use X Layer’s token-transfer route to complete the burn.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The currently edited description of Uniswap protocol-fees implementation PR 167, opened Nov. 2, estimates that roughly $300,000 of fees could be swept into the jar. It warns that restarting with the existing 2,000 UNI payment could hand the accumulated assets to the first searcher at that fixed price.

Uniswap’s collection architecture requires explicit collection calls for v3 and v4 fees, so a jar’s wallet balance alone doesn't capture everything accrued in pools.

PR 167 describes an initial threshold of 80,000 UNI, a 2,000 UNI floor and a two-day half-life. Its description estimates about 10.8 days to reach the floor.

The mechanism starts with an expensive claim and reduces the required payment over time. A searcher can act when the assets it can recover justify that payment and its costs, so competition could direct more backlog value into UNI payments instead of leaving the margin created by a low fixed threshold.

The proposed schedule remains inconsistent across the published plans: the Nov. 6 forum post says roughly six days after execution, while the reviewed implementation describes about 10.8 days.

Governance would start the decay after the TokenJar points to the new releaser. Anyone could update the threshold, and searchers could bundle that update with a release.

The setter would temporarily own the releaser, returning ownership and threshold-setting control to governance on the first update at the floor. The reviewed design removes a separate reclaim function, so elapsed time alone would not restore control.

As of Nov. 15, PR 169’s combined implementation remained a draft, listing the setter and replacement releaser deployment as blockers. The releaser and setter PRs remained open, while the official deployment registry still listed X Layer’s original releaser.

For scale, Strata Terminal reported $7.8 million in UNI burns over the preceding 30 days as of Nov. 15, and 14.1 million UNI in recurring burns overall, separate from the one-time 100 million UNI treasury burn.

That recurring flow gives the proposed one-time release context, but the older $300,000 backlog estimate uses a different observation date and valuation method. A direct conversion ratio would conflate an estimated stock of fees with realized burn flows.

The proposed repair’s economic test is how much UNI the release payment ultimately sends to Ethereum’s burn address.

#5066. 5 Tokens That Held Up Post-TGE

Home » Cryptopedia » 5 Tokens That Held Up Post-TGE

While most crypto tokens struggle to maintain their launch valuations, these five projects have demonstrated varying degrees of post-TGE price resilience.

For almost all crypto tokens, the Token Generation Event (TGE) is the best day they will ever have.

For 2026, so far only about 28% of tokens launched remained above their TGE closing price after 60 days. The median token was trading at just 0.64x its launch close.

Tokens surviving and rising in price for days, hours, and even minutes post-launch is not an easy feat. Here are five tokens that have so far avoided the typical post-TGE collapse:

Today, $1WIN is trading around $0.045, roughly 50% above its public sale price.

It is too early to compare that performance with tokens that have been trading for months or years. What makes $1WIN worth paying attention to is that its held steady in the post-TGE price rise and has a big business behind it. 1win has operated since 2016 with 100 million players and its gaming business is profitable independently of the token.

The company also plans revenue-funded open-market buybacks, while 10% of $1WIN spent through supported products will be burned. Supply is capped at one billion tokens with no additional minting.

Hyperliquid’s HYPE remains one of the clearest examples of a token that did considerably more than survive launch.

HYPE began trading in 2024 and today is hitting over $73, leaving it several multiples above its earliest market prices nearly two years later.

The underlying exchange also gives the token an economic connection to platform activity. Trading fees flow into Hyperliquid’s Assistance Fund, which acquires HYPE, with accumulated tokens subsequently burned.

That combination of product usage, revenue and token demand makes HYPE one of the stronger examples of the kind of pre-TGE traction highlighted in Decasonic’s research.

Aster launched ASTER in September 2025 as the token behind its decentralized spot and perpetuals trading platform.

Despite substantial volatility since then, ASTER was trading around $0.65. It remains well below its $2.42 all-time high, but also far above its earliest trading levels.

ASTER has not gone up in a straight line. But more than a year after launch, the token has retained a substantial part of the value created during its early growth rather than fading toward zero after TGE.

Backpack’s BP demonstrates another type of post-TGE resilience.

The token launched in March and subsequently fell as low as roughly $0.12 in May. By this month, BP had recovered to around $0.90, after reaching an all-time high of $1.66 in October.

That makes BP interesting because its strength did not come immediately after TGE. The token went through a deep early correction before rebuilding.

It also sits inside an established exchange and wallet ecosystem, with utility including trading-fee discounts, rewards, staking and access to other Backpack products.

ZAMA is trading around $0.067, approximately one-third above that public auction price.

The token also has direct utility within the Zama Protocol, where it is used to pay for encryption and decryption services and reward operators and stakers securing the network.

Stronger 2026 launches generally arrived with real usage, paying users, revenue, TVL or another measurable source of pre-TGE traction. The token was being added to something that already worked.

A strong launch can be created by hype. Holding up once the launch is over usually requires much more.

#5065. No-KYC Ecosystems: Balancing Privacy and Blockchain Verification in 2026

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Critics claim Know-Your-Customer processes are the only way to prevent fraud. Blockchain architecture proves them wrong every single day. Decentralized protocols substitute trust in centralized operators with unassailable mathematical consensus.

The technical architecture behind private, verification-first environments relies on three robust pillars:

Canadians take pride in practical common sense – we skate to where the puck is going, and the future of consumer internet tech lies squarely in self-custody and algorithmic verification.

Skipping identity verification must never mean skipping personal diligence. Rogue operators sometimes disguise shoddy infrastructure behind an anonymous facade, hoping unsuspecting users will overlook missing licenses or slow payment protocols.

Astute players examine transaction latency first. Top-tier non-custodial portals automate payout execution straight to the mempool, rejecting manual morning approvals that trap user liquidity. Furthermore, legitimate no-ID platforms support provably fair algorithms that let you inspect every single outcome on an immutable public ledger.

No-KYC entertainment networks represent much more than a convenient shortcut; they signal a permanent cultural pushback against constant digital surveillance. Users across Canada have realized that true cybersecurity starts by refusing to share unnecessary private data. As decentralized payment rails and cryptographic tools evolve, the demand for anonymous, lightning-fast interactive experiences will only accelerate. The future belongs to platforms that respect personal boundaries, safeguard sovereignty, and let mathematics do the heavy lifting.

Bitcoin was trading at $64,768 on Tuesday morning, up 1.27% over the past 24 hours, after the price of the cryptocurrency touched $62,000 over the weekend. Ether rose to $1,810, and Solana gained 3.92% to $73.18.

Exchanges liquidated $279.23 million in positions over the past 24 hours, and the amounts liquidated in longs and shorts were almost equal, according to CoinGlass.

The market value of the 473 million XRP held by the company stood at $516 million on that date, and Evernorth plans to use the reserves for yield strategies and ecosystem development.
