#4966. Grayscale says crypto pullback offers ‘compelling entry points’ as bitcoin falls to $64,000

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Grayscale Head of Research Zach Pandl said crypto still has strong fundamental demand, which makes the current prices attractive entry points for mid-to-long-term investors.

Grayscale Head of Research Zach Pandl said current crypto prices represent “compelling entry points” for medium- and longer-term investors after a sharp decline in early November.

The Block's price data show that bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell about 13% over the past week and 20.8% over the past 30 days. The GMCI Index, which measures the performance of the top 30 cryptocurrencies by market capitalization, is down 13.8% in the past seven days. This aligns with Pandl's report that the decline in large-cap assets led to the current weakness.

Pandl attributed the drop to two primary factors rather than a single catalyst. The first was stablecoin outflows into fiat currencies, which accelerated after a major security incident at Binance at the end of last month.

"Some investors may be temporarily exiting digital assets for traditional bank-based systems due to high-profile security incidents," wrote Pandl, who added that he views GENIUS Act-compliant stablecoins such as USDC as low-risk.

The second driver was spillover from AI-related equities, including Nvidia. The report said growth portfolios that hold both crypto and tech stocks cut back on risk. It also noted that the two sectors share similar infrastructure needs, such as bitcoin miners providing computing power for AI firms.

"Therefore, a deterioration in the outlook for AI investment could have implications for Bitcoin infrastructure providers and Bitcoin itself," the report said.

Technical factors amplified the move, Pandl said. Bitcoin fell below its 200-day moving average on Nov. 12, a level that was used as a "sell signal" by trend-following funds. The decline also prompted liquidations of leveraged positions in perpetual futures. Outflows from crypto exchange-traded products did not appear extreme, according to the note.

Meanwhile, Grayscale said the pullback did not appear closely tied to the planned Anthropic initial public offering or to pressures on Strategy (MSTR). Its proprietary indicators showed valuations swinging rapidly from overbought to oversold, the report added.

"Admittedly, the macro market backdrop still looks challenging, due to uncertainty around AI investment after the midterms, high inflation and the prospect of more Fed rate hikes, and geopolitical conflict," Pandl said.

However, the researcher said fundamental trends for crypto — such as institutional adoption of stablecoins and tokenized assets — remain strong. Another positive signal in the report was the ongoing deficit spending, which creates continuous demand for alternative stores of value.

"We think digital asset fundamentals look solid, and current prices represent compelling entry points," Pandl wrote.

#4965. Trump Backs Jensen Huang’s Return to Nvidia as Sam Altman Says 'Heroes Have Flaws'

Trump Backs Jensen Huang’s Return to Nvidia as Sam Altman Says 'Heroes Have Flaws'

President Donald Trump joined Elon Musk on Monday in supporting Jensen Huang’s return as Nvidia CEO, while OpenAI chief Sam Altman expressed empathy for the ousted co-founder without endorsing his reinstatement.

“I would certainly not mind seeing that,” Trump told reporters Monday as he left the White House for Asia, according to Reuters. “He could return. There’s a big group of people that would like him to return.”

Trump said Huang had “learned a big lesson,” and questioned claims that Alice Huang was a Chinese spy. “Nobody’s proven that she’s a spy,” he said, per The Guardian.

Trump also dismissed concerns that his ties with Huang threatened national security and defended Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick amid questions over enforcement of AI-chip export controls.

Musk also said Friday he was signing a petition demanding Huang’s reinstatement.

“A petition is underway to reinstate Jensen and I’m signing it too,” Musk wrote on X. Epic Games boss Tim Sweeney and Replit CEO Amjad Masad are also listed among the supporters.

More than 182,000 people had signed the open letter by Monday. It seeks Huang’s return during an independent investigation, although signatories’ identities and affiliations are not independently verified.

Speaking at Semafor’s Silicon Valley & The World conference Monday, Altman said Huang had “long been a hero of mine. Heroes have flaws.” He described feeling “personal strong empathy” for the former Nvidia boss.

He has reason to understand a boardroom revolt. OpenAI fired Altman in November 2023, only to reinstate him less than five days later after employee and investor backlash. Still, asked whether Huang should return, Altman said: “I don’t think I know enough to answer that question.”

Altman said he struggled to imagine Nvidia without Huang, but argued the giant chipmaker was big enough to retain investor trust. That is sympathy—not an endorsement.

Huang, who founded Nvidia in 1993, stepped down Thursday after admitting infidelity. Reports later linked him to Alice Huang and two Nvidia employees, one a subordinate.

Alice Huang previously worked at Megaspeed, a Singapore-based Nvidia customer facing scrutiny over possible diversion of advanced chips to China. Megaspeed denies wrongdoing; espionage allegations remain unproven.

Washington is tightening export controls on advanced AI chips, including new licensing requirements for shipments to Singapore and Malaysia, as officials seek to prevent diversion to China.

Trump’s backing also puts him at odds with Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent, who said Sunday that Nvidia should look outside its ranks for a replacement. “They need a dramatic shake-up,” Bessent told Fox News.

Nvidia’s board has made no decision to reinstate Huang. CFO Colette Kress is serving as interim CEO, while the Financial Times reports Huang’s allies are lobbying for his return and Arm CEO Rene Haas is a potential successor.

Employees have also pushed for reinstatement. An internal discussion drew more than 1,600 comments, including calls for Huang’s return.

Nvidia shares opened 2.7% lower Monday, wiping out roughly $100 billion in market value at the open.

Huang could face congressional questions on China chip exports in early December. Nvidia reports earnings Tuesday, its first financial update under Kress.

#4963. Twenty One Capital (XXI) Buys 154 Bitcoin, Its First Purchase of 2026

Twenty One Capital bought 154.32 bitcoin for about $10 million in cash after the weekend selloff, lifting its holdings to about 43,668 BTC.

Twenty One Capital bought 154.32 bitcoin for about $10 million in cash after the weekend selloff, the company said Monday, its first disclosed purchase of 2026 and a small addition to a treasury that had barely moved all year.

The Tether-controlled company said it paid an average of about $64,801 per coin, bringing its holdings to about 43,668 BTC as of Nov. 16. At Tuesday morning’s price near $64,900, that stack is worth roughly $2.83 billion.

“Bitcoin is our benchmark for every investment decision we make. Any business we acquire has to beat simply buying more Bitcoin, and this weekend’s drop in price made adding Bitcoin an easy decision,” CEO Rapha Zagury said in the release.

“Every dollar we spend, whether on Bitcoin, acquisitions, or buybacks, is weighed against maintaining flexibility to run our business well,” he added.

The buy breaks a long pause. Twenty One’s quarterly report, filed Nov. 13, shows it held 43,515 BTC at the end of 2025 and 43,514 BTC on Sept. 30. The only change in between was a single bitcoin transferred on March 31 as a vendor payment.

The same filing shows how much room the company has to keep buying with cash. Twenty One reported $102.8 million in cash and equivalents at Sept. 30, down from $117.7 million at the end of 2025. The $10 million purchase equals about a tenth of that September balance, although the company has not said how much cash it holds now.

Twenty One also carries $486.5 million of convertible notes issued when it went public. Those notes are secured by about 16,116 BTC pledged as collateral, coins the filing says are not available for general corporate purposes while they back the debt.

The company’s bitcoin cost basis was about $3.69 billion at Sept. 30, or roughly $84,900 per coin. The new coins came in about $20,000 below that average, but the treasury as a whole remains underwater at current prices.

Twenty One’s Class A shares closed at $4.56 on Monday, down 7.9% on the day, before the purchase was announced after the market close. The 10-Q lists 346.65 million Class A shares outstanding, which at that price are worth about $1.58 billion, well below the market value of the company’s bitcoin.

That gap has been the central problem for Zagury since he replaced founder Jack Mallers as CEO in July. In an August shareholder letter, he acknowledged investor concerns about the stock trading at a discount to its bitcoin and said Twenty One “must become more than a Bitcoin treasury.” Tether took control of the company in May after buying out SoftBank’s stake.

The company’s latest shareholder letter, released Nov. 13 with third-quarter results, laid out a plan to build cash-generating businesses around the bitcoin treasury. Twenty One reported third-quarter net income of $1.087 billion, almost all of it from a $1.093 billion gain in the fair value of its bitcoin as prices rose during the quarter. For the first nine months of 2026, it posted a net loss of $186.5 million.

Twenty One said further bitcoin purchases are core to its strategy, with the amount and timing set by market conditions, available liquidity and competing investment opportunities. The company did not commit to a size or schedule.

The purchase landed the same day Strategy (MSTR), the largest corporate holder, disclosed buying 485 BTC for $33.4 million, lifting its holdings to 850,849 BTC.

The test now is whether those purchases stay opportunistic and small, or whether Twenty One uses bitcoin, acquisitions or buybacks to close the gap between its share price and its coins.

#4959. Clarity Act Defeat ‘Failure of American Leadership’: Trump’s Former Crypto Chief

Clarity Act Defeat ‘Failure of American Leadership’: Trump’s Former Crypto Chief

The Senate's failure to pass the Clarity Act was "a failure of American leadership," according to Patrick Witt, President Donald Trump's former top crypto adviser.

"It was extremely disappointing," Witt said in an on-stage interview at Solana's Breakpoint conference in London. "I was knee deep in Clarity. I lived it for a year plus negotiating that bill," he noted, adding that the bill, "would have been incredibly impactful."

Witt served as executive director of the President's Council of Advisers for Digital Assets until last month and led the White House push for the bill, which would have set out a framework for regulating crypto markets. It failed a key procedural vote in the Senate in September.

"We ultimately lost against the clock," he said, adding that, "The policy was good. We resolved pretty much all of the policy differences." But with the midterm elections approaching, "the political issue of ethics really consumed everything else," he said.

Democrats had raised concerns over the Trump family's crypto ventures. Witt acknowledged that Trump's involvement in crypto was "arguably the issue," but said the president had agreed to two ethics provisions. The second would have barred federal officials, including the president, from issuing or sponsoring digital assets, forced the president to divest certain digital assets or put them in a blind trust, and let state attorneys general enforce violations. "There's no precedent for those kinds of things in American law," he said.

The GENIUS stablecoin bill also lost an initial procedural vote in 2025 but recovered because it came early in Trump's term, Witt noted. With Clarity, "we knew that we were going to get one shot at it, and unfortunately we just came up short," he said.

Looking ahead, Witt said the odds of passing a comprehensive replacement are low. "I would expect that market structure in the United States is going to be less written on Capitol Hill and more written in the Federal Register, which is where the agencies do their rulemakings and issue their guidance," Witt said.

Parts of the Clarity Act could still pass in larger legislative packages, and lawmakers could grant targeted powers, such as giving the Commodity Futures Trading Commission authority over spot crypto markets, he said.

Witt said regulators already have "ample authority," and that the administration had to "hold them back a little bit" while the bill played out. The SEC and CFTC have since moved ahead with their own crypto rules, though House Financial Services Committee Chairman French Hill (R-AR) has said those efforts fall short of what the legislation would have achieved.

SEC Chair Paul Atkins and CFTC Chair Michael Selig "are committed to moving markets on chain," Witt said, framing the issue as one of American competitiveness. "Capital is going to go where it's treated best," he said.

Despite Democrats winning control of both chambers of Congress in this month's midterms, Witt said he is "not bearish on the industry overall," since the regulators and the president remain in place. Democrats are reportedly planning to push their own, broader market structure bill.

He said opposition to crypto is increasingly generational rather than purely partisan. While some Democrats, such as Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA), are "going to be 100% opposed to anything that seemingly is favorable to crypto," younger lawmakers on both sides see it as "part of the fabric of financial infrastructure," he said, pointing to Sens. Angela Alsobrooks (D-MD) and Ruben Gallego (D-AZ).

Witt left the administration in October, The Hill reported. He said he has held talks with crypto firms, venture capital firms and defense companies, and hopes to announce his next move in the coming weeks.

#4957. Bitcoin Policy Institute Renames to Finney Foundation for Open Technology

The Bitcoin Policy Institute announced Tuesday that it is now the Finney Foundation for Open Technology, a new parent organization whose brief spans open-source artificial intelligence, digital cash and encrypted messaging. The institute’s bitcoin work continues inside the foundation, and bitcoin remains a primary focus.

The name honors Hal Finney (1956-2014), a cryptographer and software developer who helped build the email encryption program PGP, ran one of the first anonymous remailers and received the first bitcoin transaction from Satoshi Nakamoto in January 2009. In a November 1992 essay, Finney wrote that the computer “can be used as a tool to liberate and protect people, rather than to control them.” He was diagnosed with ALS the same year he received that first transaction and died in 2014.

Washington is now writing the rules for artificial intelligence, the foundation said, and fear drives much of that debate. As the Bitcoin Policy Institute did for bitcoin, the Finney Foundation said it will argue that open systems outcompete closed ones, that individuals should control the tools they rely on, and that American strength comes from building in the open.

“We set out to make Washington think about Bitcoin differently — to educate them on the importance of open monetary networks,” said David Zell, president of the Finney Foundation. “Our fellows now serve in congressional offices for both parties and in the executive branch. Our ideas have shaped discourse and policy. Our expansion will help us stay at the forefront of today’s most consequential policy debates, especially those around open source AI.”

That argument was already on stage in September at Freedom Tech DC, the institute’s Washington summit, formerly the Bitcoin Policy Summit. The invite-only gathering put open-source and open-weight AI, data centers and energy infrastructure on the agenda alongside bitcoin and dollar-backed stablecoins.

Block co-founder Jack Dorsey said there that “the next and probably largest element [of freedom technology] is open intelligence and making sure that it’s not locked up in the hands of any one government or any one company… that’s where we’re going to get the best outcomes.”

Alongside the new name, the foundation published an Open Technology Declaration, titled “For Us, Not Over Us,” which argues for machines that serve people rather than rule over them. The text takes up Finney’s line about computers as tools of liberation and says the foundation will offer research, policy ideas and educational programs aimed at that goal. It asks who the new machines are for and who is in control of them, and it calls for people to build the tools they need without waiting for permission.

It is also launching an inaugural class of Finney Fellows, researchers with expertise spanning machine intelligence, free speech, national security, biotechnology and space. The foundation says that cohort will be named Wednesday at 10 a.m. ET. A separate roster of Bitcoin Policy Institute fellows remains listed on the same page.

The shift does not retire the institute’s bitcoin brief. The Bitcoin Policy Institute, founded in 2022, has run a congressional fellowship program to place bitcoin specialists in congressional offices, joined the State Department’s Freedom Tech Excellence Program as a founding partner, and urged Congress to include bitcoin in proposed de minimis tax relief. In August, it named Conner Brown executive director as it expanded its leadership team.

The Finney Foundation describes itself as a nonpartisan, nonprofit 501(c)(3) think tank in Washington, D.C. Zell is president. Brown remains executive director on the new staff list. For now, the bitcoin think tank still exists, just under a wider roof, with a new class of fellows due to be named in the morning.

#4955. Former Robinhood engineer pleads guilty to wire fraud over trades ahead of crypto listing announcements

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Xiang admitted to using confidential information about planned Robinhood Crypto listings and agreed to forfeit $125,523 in trading proceeds.

Huaisong Xiang, a former Robinhood Robinhood Markets, Inc. (HOOD) software engineer, pleaded guilty to wire fraud after using confidential information about planned cryptocurrency listings to trade perpetual futures on Hyperliquid.

Xiang agreed to forfeit $125,523 in proceeds from the scheme as part of his plea, the U.S. Attorney’s Office for the Southern District of New York said in a statement on Monday.

According to the statement, Xiang made the trades between 2025 and 2026, ahead of Robinhood's public announcements that those tokens would be listed on Robinhood Crypto.

Prosecutors said Xiang knew about the planned listings before each trade but used that information for his own benefit, making $125,523 in the process.

Xiang was charged in September alongside former Robinhood engineer Hefu Chai. Prosecutors alleged the two separately used confidential information about planned listings to trade token perpetuals on Hyperliquid, with each making more than $50,000.

According to the criminal complaints, Robinhood’s policy barred designated employees from trading on any platform before and for 24 hours after the company publicly announced a new crypto listing or delisting.

Xiang initially denied the charges. His attorney told Forbes in September that his client disputed the allegations. Robinhood told Bloomberg it had investigated the matter and reported it to law enforcement and regulators.

“Robinhood takes market integrity seriously and has zero tolerance for insider trading,” the company said at the time, adding that it had policies covering new crypto listings and would continue cooperating with investigators.

Xiang is scheduled to be sentenced on Feb. 25, 2027. The wire fraud count carries a maximum sentence of 20 years in prison.

#4953. Lighter reopens withdrawals after trader’s API key leak, returns about $5 million in USDC

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Transfers into Lighter's public pools now require a wallet signature, and API key signatures have been made non-public.

Lighter's Security Council has returned about $5 million in USDC to a trader whose API keys were compromised on Monday, the exchange said in a postmortem.

The Ethereum-based perpetual futures exchange initially reported a suspected compromise involving one external party and suspended withdrawals and pool creation as a precaution. It later identified a leaked API key for a specific trader's account and said recovering funds from an additional withdrawal attempt was its priority. The team expected to complete contract upgrades and reopen withdrawals by the end of Monday. In its postmortem, Lighter said the anomaly had triggered its smart contract Guardian, whose temporary pause on withdrawals allowed the Security Council to return the funds.

"Deposits and withdrawals are back to normal following the deactivation of Guardian Mode by the Lighter Security Council," Lighter announced later that day.

Lighter Lighter (LIT) did not disclose the total size of the theft or the name of the affected trader, though onchain analysts Lookonchain and Onchain Lens estimated losses at $15.5 million and suggested that the affected account belonged to Abraxas Capital.

According to Lighter's post-mortem report, the attacker opened a public pool from the compromised account, then minted shares in a separate account they controlled. They then transferred the trader's money into the pool without minting more shares, increasing the value of the shares the attacker held, which they redeemed before withdrawing, Lighter said.

Lighter said the API keys were leaked through the account's own signatures, using signing software that came from outside its official software development kits, and reused nonces, the one-time values required for signatures. This meant that anyone could calculate the private key from two public signatures if both used the same nonce and key, per the report. Lighter said its official SDKs, protocol, smart contracts, and infrastructure were not affected.

Following the incident, Lighter said an API key alone can no longer authorize a transfer to a public pool. Lighter now requires a wallet signature and has made API key signatures non-public. The team said it was also adding IP allowlists and permissions for individual keys, including restrictions on which markets a key can trade.

#4951. Safe wallet reportedly loses $6.5M as Zodiac suspects key compromise

Zodiac’s co-founder said a likely compromised key and overly permissive wallet settings enabled the theft, rather than a flaw in its smart contracts.

A Safe wallet reportedly lost about $6.45 million in cryptocurrency Monday, with Zodiac’s co-founder pointing to a likely compromised key and the wallet’s permissions settings rather than a flaw in Zodiac’s contracts.

Blockchain security firm PeckShield reported the suspected theft, citing onchain investigator Specter. Ethereum blockchain records reviewed by Cointelegraph show that approximately 4.39 million USDC (USDC) and 1.72 million syrupUSDC tokens were transferred from the wallet in a single transaction on Monday.

Johannes Moormann, co-founder of Zodiac, told Cointelegraph that his team reviewed the transaction and confirmed it passed through the wallet’s Zodiac Roles Modifier, which checked the transfer against its configured permissions policy and allowed it. “There is no vulnerability in the module. The contracts worked correctly. The transaction came from the key of a long-standing role member, which was most likely compromised,” he said.

Moormann added that the wallet’s custom permissions policy permitted the transaction, almost certainly unintentionally. He said the wallet’s operators controlled its keys and permissions, and that Zodiac had neither access to them nor involvement in the setup. Zodiac has not been in contact with the affected team.

Specter first flagged the Zodiac Roles module as part of the suspected attack. PeckShield also said the attacker used approximately $813,000 to purchase Monero-linked tokens on Hyperliquid.

The Zodiac Roles module allows Safe users to assign permissions for specific transactions. Moormann said the incident did not indicate a vulnerability affecting other wallets using the module.

The identity of the wallet owner has not been established. Cointelegraph contacted Safe and Gnosis Guild for comment but had not received a response by publication.

Related: DOJ moves to seize $1.2B from Binance hack, disrupts FaceStealer scam

Blockchain analytics firm Bitquery traced funds from the Safe wallet through multiple addresses to Hyperliquid, where five accounts purchased approximately $1 million in XMR1 tokens.

The firm noted similarities with fund movements following Monday’s Lighter incident, including a 160 Ether (ETH) transfer and purchases of XMR1.

However, Bitquery said it found no transfers connecting the two wallet groups and could not establish whether the same actor was involved.

#4950. Goldman Sachs More Than Doubles Its Bitcoin ETF Holdings to $1.28 Billion

Goldman Sachs more than doubled its spot bitcoin ETF holdings in the third quarter to about $1.28 billion, driven by a 52-fold jump in Fidelity’s fund, while cutting its stake in Strategy (MSTR) by more than half, a Monday filing shows.

The bank reported the positions in a Form 13F covering the quarter ended Sept. 30. Its long holdings across five spot bitcoin funds were valued at $1.283 billion, up from about $580 million in its filing for June 30, an increase of roughly 121%.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust remained Goldman’s largest bitcoin fund holding, at 16,440,599 shares worth $778.3 million. The share count actually slipped about 5% from 17,232,201 at midyear. The value rose because IBIT climbed from $33.29 to $47.34 a share during the quarter, a gain of about 42%.

The bigger change was in the Fidelity Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund. Goldman held 5,477,583 FBTC shares worth $397.9 million at Sept. 30, up from just 105,840 shares, worth $5.4 million, three months earlier.

Goldman also reported 2,000,020 shares of the Morgan Stanley Bitcoin Trust, worth $47.9 million. The fund, ticker MSBT, launched in April as the first bitcoin product from a U.S. bank-affiliated asset manager, with a 0.14% sponsor fee. Goldman’s June filing did not list it. The trust reported 36.42 million shares outstanding and $873.4 million in net assets at Sept. 30, so Goldman held about 5.5% of the fund.

The rest of the bitcoin fund book was smaller. Grayscale’s Bitcoin Mini Trust rose to 1,383,498 shares, worth $51.1 million, from 22,905. Goldman also listed 120,600 shares of the Grayscale Bitcoin Trust, worth $7.8 million, alongside puts on the same number of shares, which suggests that position is hedged.

The bank’s options book in IBIT is large, too. It reported puts on 7,513,000 IBIT shares, valued at $355.7 million, and calls on 4,895,600 shares, valued at $231.8 million. Those figures reflect the value of the underlying shares, not premiums paid, so they do not measure money at risk. Goldman runs one of Wall Street’s biggest market-making and derivatives businesses, and its 13F does not say which positions are hedges for clients and which are its own views.

The bank went the other way on Strategy. Goldman’s long position in the bitcoin treasury company fell to 2,760,766 shares, worth $422.6 million at Sept. 30, from 5,810,162 shares at midyear, a drop of about 52%.

Its Strategy put options barely moved, covering 4,738,700 shares worth $725.4 million, compared with 4,735,800 in June. Calls rose to 707,200 shares from 575,900. Strategy closed at $103.03 on Monday, down from $153.09 at the end of September, as the company keeps buying bitcoin and buying back preferred stock.

The pattern mirrors the one at Wells Fargo, whose own 13F, also filed Monday, showed its IBIT stake more than doubling while its Strategy position fell by about a third. JPMorgan reported $540 million in bitcoin ETFs in its third-quarter filing last week.

The Goldman positions are a snapshot as of Sept. 30, before bitcoin’s fall from above $83,000 to about $62,000 over the weekend. At Monday’s closing price of $36.79, the bank’s 16.4 million IBIT shares would be worth about $605 million, roughly $173 million less than the value in the filing.

U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs have been bleeding money since the quarter ended. IBIT lost about $2 billion over nine straight sessions through mid-November before taking in roughly $76.5 million on Monday.

The filing does not show whether Goldman added to or trimmed its bitcoin fund positions in October or November. That answer arrives with the fourth-quarter 13F, due in mid-February.

#4949. Tom Lee Blames Nvidia and Anthropic IPO for Ethereum Plunge as Bitmine Buys the Dip

Tom Lee Blames Nvidia and Anthropic IPO for Ethereum Plunge as Bitmine Buys the Dip

Bitmine Immersion Technologies kept buying Ethereum through a punishing selloff, adding 7,653 ETH worth roughly $14 million, as chairman Tom Lee argued the token’s steep decline runs counter to improving fundamentals and echoes a dip that preceded a major tech IPO earlier this year.

The NYSE-listed company said Monday its holdings reached 6,077,745 ETH, worth about $11.1 billion at a reference price of $1,823 per coin, far below where the firm’s stack was valued just weeks ago.

That marks a stark drop in paper value: Bitmine’s total crypto, cash and “moonshot” holdings now stand at $11.8 billion, down from $14.3 billion a week earlier, almost entirely because of ETH’s price slide rather than any selling.

The position still represents 4.97% of Ethereum’s 122.2 million token supply, leaving Bitmine 99% of the way to its goal of owning 5% of the network, the target it calls the “Alchemy of 5%.”

Lee did not sugarcoat the move. “ETH tumbled 28% in the past two weeks,” he said, calling the “waterfall decline” inconsistent with what he views as positive fundamentals, including banks and financial institutions announcing tokenization projects built on Ethereum. (Disclosure: Tom Lee is an investor in Dastan, Decrypt’s parent company.)

He pointed to external pressures weighing on the price, including the fallout from Nvidia’s turmoil triggering margin selling in Asia and investors raising cash ahead of Anthropic’s initial public offering, whose pricing is reported for Nov. 18.

Lee also drew a historical parallel, arguing the drop mirrors the sell-off ahead of SpaceX’s $75 billion June IPO, when ETH fell 39% over eight weeks and bottomed three trading days before pricing. Lee suggested the current slump, down a similar amount ahead of the Anthropic listing, could follow the same pattern.

Bitmine remains the world’s largest Ethereum treasury and the second-largest crypto treasury overall, behind Michael Saylor’s Strategy, which now holds 849,327 BTC. Strategy also kept buying through the downturn, with Saylor nudging the market to rally with the quip “this dip could use a little orange.”

Bitmine has 5,227,037 ETH, about 86% of its holdings, staked through its MAVAN platform, with projected annualized revenue of roughly $245 million at current levels.

It also holds 216 Bitcoin, $448 million in cash and marketable securities, a $180 million stake in Beast Industries and a $67 million position in Eightco Holdings.

#4947. Grvt launches RWA perpetuals with funding tied to traditional markets

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Market makers submit funding rates based on their financing costs in traditional markets, and Grvt adds a markup.

Grvt, a hybrid exchange that runs on its own ZKsync-based Ethereum Layer 2, has launched perpetual contracts tied to real-world assets that link funding rates to market makers’ financing costs in traditional markets.

Dubbed Stable Funding Perps, the product initially covers seven commodity markets, with funding settled in USDC once each weekday at market close, according to a statement shared with The Block.

Rates vary for long and short positions and adjust for holidays and weekend financing, the platform said, adding that traders can pay or receive funding depending on prevailing rates.

Grvt GRVT Token (GRVT) said the contracts use a request-for-quote model where market makers provide live buy and sell quotes while managing their hedges through traditional financial markets. The platform charges no trading fee, while execution costs are added to quoted prices.

The initial rollout carries larger minimum trade sizes, although there are plans to enable 24/7 availability and support smaller minimums over time, per the statement. Most contracts trade about 23 hours a day, five days a week, following the CME Globex calendar and market holidays to align with underlying reference pricing.

"We don't believe every onchain market needs to recreate liquidity that already exists elsewhere," Grvt CEO Hong Yea said in the statement. "If market makers can hedge more efficiently in established markets, they can pass those efficiencies on through better pricing and deeper liquidity."

Grvt raised $19 million in Series A funding in September 2025 as it expanded its platform into areas including options and real-world assets.

In August, the platform also announced a plan to build a $100 million position in Ondo Finance's USDY, a tokenized secured note backed primarily by short-term U.S. Treasurys.

Standard Chartered said Monday that the RWA market, currently about $45 billion, could reach $2 trillion by the end of 2028, with longer-dated government bonds providing another potential source of growth.

Grvt plans to expand SFPs into equities and other RWA markets, with an eventual target of more than 100 markets, according to the statement.

#4945. Nvidia turmoil tests Bitcoin miners’ AI funding plans

Nvidia’s leadership upheaval and Texas permit restrictions sharpen the divide between miners with funded projects and those still seeking capital and approvals.

Jensen Huang’s departure from Nvidia comes as Bitcoin miners are committing billions of dollars to risky AI infrastructure pivots.

For investors, questions remain around whether large AI contracts can produce cash flows that justify construction costs and support debt service and lead to a sustainable business model. The pivot replaces exposure to Bitcoin (BTC) with construction, financing and customer credit risks. Companies with already-funded projects, usable power and enforceable payment commitments could therefore deliver sharply different returns from peers whose expansion still depends on additional capital and regulatory approvals.

Huang’s departure reaches miners through Nvidia’s overlapping roles as supplier, customer and financier. IREN signed a five-year, $3.4 billion cloud services contract with Nvidia in May, while Nvidia invested $2 billion in CoreWeave in January.

IREN and Applied Digital fell during the Nov. 12 AI selloff following Huang’s departure. For companies seeking equity financing, lower share prices mean issuing more shares to raise the same amount.

JPMorgan credit analyst Erica Spear said “new deals could take longer or require more compensation.” For miners, a slowdown in Nvidia investments, guarantees or capacity purchases could raise financing costs and delay construction. Tighter funding for their cloud-provider tenants could also weaken demand for additional capacity.

Related: Core Scientific to quit Bitcoin mining by year-end as AI business surges

A financing retreat has not been confirmed. Nvidia’s board said it expects operations, customer commitments and its product roadmap to continue uninterrupted.

It is also important to recognize that the AI data center industry is in fact a diverse set of entirely separate business models with their own risk exposures. Leasing powered land, operating colocation facilities, and owning GPUs to sell computing services have different construction requirements, operating responsibilities and equipment replacement exposure.

#4943. Stablecoin infrastructure firm Avenia raises $25 million in Series A extension

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Lightspeed Faction led the equity round, bringing Avenia's total funding to $44.6 million as it plans new products and acquisitions.

Avenia, a Brazilian stablecoin infrastructure firm, has raised $25 million in a Series A extension round as it plans to launch new products and looks for acquisitions.

"The terms made a lot of sense, so we opened an extension of $25 million," Noel said.

The extension follows a $17 million round announced in February, bringing funding this year to $42 million. Including its pre-seed and seed rounds, Avenia has raised $44.6 million since its founding in 2023, Noel said.

He declined to disclose the valuation, but said it was "a very significant step-up from our previous round."

Founded as BRLA Digital and based in São Paulo, Avenia lets businesses receive, convert, hold, and send funds between local currencies and stablecoins through an API or dashboard. It also issues BRLA, a stablecoin backed by the Brazilian real. The company reports 7 billion reais (about $1.2 billion) in monthly transaction volume for BRLA.

Most of Avenia's clients are in Brazil, but the company supports payouts to more than 100 countries, Noel said. It uses payment systems including SWIFT, ACH, wire transfers, and SEPA internationally, and Pix, TED, and boleto in Brazil.

Its clients include companies managing international treasury operations; investment advisers, brokerages, securities distributors, wealth managers and family offices; and banks and financial institutions seeking technology to offer virtual asset services, Noel said.

Products under development include merchant acquiring, a product offering returns on Brazilian real balances, and a Visa card program. Merchant acquiring is due to launch this year. The yield product will start with four existing clients, while the card is in the final design approval stage with Visa, Noel said.

"They're all at an advanced stage," he noted. "There's more on the way, but we'd rather share it as each one goes live."

Avenia is allowed to operate as a virtual asset service provider while its application is under review under Brazil's central bank Resolution 520, Noel said. Its final license is still pending.

New products require regulatory approval, making licenses part of Avenia's acquisition plans, Noel said. It is looking at payment institutions, including electronic money issuers, as well as brokerages, securities distributors known as DTVMs, and possibly banks. Companies licensed elsewhere in Latin America or the U.S. could also be targets, he added.

"The idea is complementarity, not competition: to verticalize Avenia and expand the products we can offer," he said.

Avenia currently has 43 employees, almost three times its headcount at the start of the year, and expects to reach 50 by year-end, Noel said.

#4942. Hackers Redirected Bitcoin Miner Rewards via AI Attack, Luxor CEO Says

Hackers Redirected Bitcoin Miner Rewards via AI Attack, Luxor CEO Says

Hackers briefly hijacked computing power from Bitcoin miners using Luxor’s fleet management software, in what the company’s CEO called an AI-enabled attack.

Luxor Technology said Sunday that its internal monitoring flagged an unauthorized redirect of hashrate through Commander, its tool for managing mining machines.

Hashrate is the computing power miners devote to securing Bitcoin and earning rewards. Just over a dozen client accounts were affected, with their machines pointed to a different mining pool for a limited window, effectively sending the value of their work elsewhere.

Luxor said it patched Commander within two hours and began restoring affected machines to their original settings. No data was leaked or taken from its systems, and the issue was isolated to Commander, according to the company.

Luxor Pool and its LuxOS software weren’t affected, the company said, adding that every affected client will be paid in full for the redirected hashrate, as if their machines had mined normally.

Luxor CEO Nick Hansen said the compromise began around 6 p.m. Pacific time Saturday through “a sophisticated AI-enabled vector.” He didn’t detail how the attack worked. “You trust us with your fleet, and we need to be better,” he wrote on X.

Around 6:00p PDT yesterday, a subset of Commander customers were compromised, through a sophisticated AI-enabled vector. We were able to identify and patch quickly so that it didn't continue, then the hard work of reverting started.…

In a follow-up post, Hansen said crypto businesses are now “inundated with AI-enabled attacks,” with abliterated models, or AI models stripped of their safety guardrails, probing systems around the clock for weaknesses.

Luxor has responded by buying its own bare-metal GPUs to run such models defensively. It has also put most new feature work on hold to harden its infrastructure by rotating keys, updating kernels and pinning dependencies. Hansen urged other companies running similar services to do the same.

Bitcoin developers are grappling with the same threat. Bitcoin Core updated its security policy to address AI-driven threats, and an AI “red team” effort has turned up critical vulnerabilities throughout the year.

The incident also follows other attacks on Bitcoin infrastructure. Blockstream’s Liquid Network restored full Bitcoin backing earlier this month, two months after a $320 million exploit.

Luxor said it would soon publish a full post-mortem of the hack.

#4937. Coinbase and Broadridge propose standard for tokenized stock disclosures

The proposed standard aims to connect tokenized stockholders with corporate disclosures and voting materials across digital wallets, but would not create shareholder rights.

Coinbase and Broadridge proposed a technical standard Monday that would let digital wallets find and display shareholder notices linked to tokenized stocks.

The jointly developed Onchain Rights Standard would give issuers, custodians and wallet providers a shared format for communications about dividends, shareholder meetings and other corporate actions. Broadridge separately announced a supporting data service, known as an oracle, intended to help participating firms document the distribution of those notices.

The proposal’s technical documentation describes an onchain announcement that links a token to a standardized corporate document. A dividend notice, for example, could specify the date, payment terms and any choices available to holders. Compatible wallets could then display notices from different issuers using the same format, reducing the need for multiple other integrations.

“The proposed standard is a communications framework; it does not itself create shareholder rights,” Mark Nichols, Broadridge’s co-president of digital assets, told Cointelegraph.

Nichols said implementation would depend on the underlying structure of each tokenized stock, including whether holders have direct shareholder rights.

The companies said they submitted the proposal for review as an Ethereum Improvement Proposal. They did not name additional adopters of the standard or give an implementation timetable.

Broadridge launched its onchain shareholder governance platform in April, naming Galaxy as an intended user for its May shareholder meeting. The platform integrates Broadridge’s ProxyVote system with digital wallets, allowing investors to receive shareholder materials, confirm holdings and submit votes, with voting records maintained on an Avalanche-based blockchain.

#4936. Bitwise launches ZDAY, its first non-crypto ETF, to bet on AI cyber defense

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The crypto asset manager's new fund will trade on the NYSE and carries a 0.75% management fee and targets 10 to 30 holdings.

Bitwise Asset Management launched the actively managed Bitwise AI Cyber Defense ETF, which invests in companies developing defenses against AI-enabled cyberattacks and tools to secure AI systems. The fund marks the crypto asset manager's first exchange-traded product outside of digital assets.

The fund began trading on the NYSE under the ticker ZDAY. It carries a 0.75% management fee and targets 10 to 30 holdings, including Palo Alto Networks, CrowdStrike, Cloudflare, and Zscaler.

"Crypto has lived at the leading edge of cybersecurity since its inception," Bitwise CIO Matt Hougan said in a release. "We've watched a multibillion-dollar security industry assemble around it in five years. The same thing is now happening to AI, on a much larger scale."

The growing security risks associated with AI also helped shape Bitwise's decision to launch the fund, with the company pointing to a July incident involving Hugging Face as an example.

"We realized the playing field was going to change dramatically," CEO Hunter Horsley told The Block. "Not just for crypto, but for the entirety of the internet."

In July, Hugging Face, a platform for building, sharing, and deploying AI models and applications, disclosed that an autonomous AI agent had intruded into its systems. Five days later, OpenAI said its own models were responsible: they had broken in while being tested internally for cybersecurity capabilities, with some safeguards loosened for the test.

"ZDAY is simply a product we wanted ourselves and thought should exist," Horsley said. "We couldn't think of a good reason we shouldn't do it. Or that a legacy asset manager would create something better."

Bitwise manages more than $14 billion in client assets, with a suite of cryptocurrency ETFs covering bitcoin, ether, XRP, Hyperliquid, and other digital assets.

#4935. Bitcoin's $2 Billion Liquidation Weekend Leaves Traders Watching $60,000

Bitcoin's $2 Billion Liquidation Weekend Leaves Traders Watching $60,000

Bitcoin fell to around $62,100 on Sunday as roughly $2 billion in crypto derivatives positions were forcibly closed, mostly bets that prices would rise. The weekend slide sent the cryptocurrency to its lowest level since July 14, adding another ugly chapter to six consecutive weeks of losses.

Bitcoin had recovered to around $64,300 early Monday. But the options market is showing why traders aren't necessarily treating that bounce as an all-clear.

Liquidations happen when an exchange closes a trader's position because the money posted to support a borrowed bet is no longer enough to cover its losses. CoinGlass figures from Saturday showed $1.9 billion liquidated in one 24-hour period, about $1.8 billion of it bullish positions across cryptocurrencies, not just Bitcoin.

U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs had already been losing investors before the weekend. Data from Decrypt’s ETF tracker show $185.4 million in net outflows on Friday, extending their losing streak to seven trading sessions and bringing withdrawals since November 5 to approximately $1.91 billion.

BlackRock's IBIT accounted for $143.5 million of Friday's withdrawals, while Fidelity's FBTC lost $41.9 million. The funds' earlier weakness had helped explain the broader exodus, although ETF flow totals measure fund creations and redemptions rather than every trade between investors.

Those numbers also come with a timing distinction: U.S. ETFs do not trade over the weekend, while Bitcoin trades around the clock. Friday's outflows show weakened demand before the crash, not that ETF shareholders were redeeming shares during Sunday's plunge.

Bitcoin's technical indicators suggest the selling may be overstretched, but that doesn't necessarily mean the worst is over.

The daily Relative Strength Index sat near 24 in oversold territory on the daily charts. Meanwhile, the Average Directional Index stood above 33, indicating a strong prevailing trend, with bearish directional pressure still dominating. The weekly RSI, near 38, pointed to a weak longer-term picture, although it had not reached oversold territory.

The cryptocurrency bounced after approaching support around $62,000. A recovery would face potential resistance near $66,500 to $67,200, followed by another obstacle around $69,300, based on the chart's Fibonacci levels that measure support and resistance marks.

A decisive break below $62,000, meanwhile, would put the psychologically important $60,000 level back in focus—precisely where options traders have accumulated significant downside exposure.

Bitcoin's next test will be whether fresh U.S. ETF flow data show more withdrawals for Monday and whether traders keep paying for downside protection. The heavily watched November 27 options expiration is still ahead, with $50,000 puts among the contracts drawing the most recent trading activity.

#4931. Solana’s Trump memecoin launch tested infrastructure ahead of institutional push, Lily Liu says

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Liu said the launch tested Solana's capacity under heavy activity, with the network reaching 60,000 transactions per second during a marketwide liquidation wave.

Solana Solana (SOL) Foundation President Lily Liu said the January 2025 launch of the TRUMP memecoin and the October 2025 crypto liquidation wave served as live stress tests for the network’s financial infrastructure ahead of its institutional momentum.

Speaking to The Block's Gareth Jenkinson at Solana Breakpoint in London, Liu described the token's launch as the decade's biggest trading event, arguing that the network's decentralized infrastructure handled the activity even as centralized financial platforms struggled to keep pace.

She said trading began on a Friday evening and continued through a weekend when traditional markets were closed, with Solana's validators, wallets, liquidity pools, and transaction routers processing activity independently.

“You can't stage a test like that of infrastructure,” Liu said. "To be literally pounced upon on a Friday evening and for all of that to go perfectly seamlessly.”

Liu acknowledged that investors have different views about memecoins as an asset class but argued that their trading activity should also be considered separately from the infrastructure processing it.

"You're not going to test that infrastructure with Apple stock, you're just not going to," she said.

She also pointed to the challenge of supporting potentially thousands of new assets, with unpredictable trading activity around the clock.

Liu also recapped another major test she said Solana faced during the Oct. 10, 2025, crypto market selloff, when approximately $20 billion in positions were liquidated across the market within two hours. The network briefly reached 60,000 transactions per second during the episode without experiencing any downtime.

"Last year when there was the 10/10 event — that was a big stress, it was a very stressful event for everywhere in blockchain," she said. "But then if you looked at how Solana handled it, nothing went down."

Liu said infrastructure could only be properly tested when markets were live, with trading activity unfolding at a scale no one could reliably predict.

Liu's comments come as traditional finance institutions are advancing their onchain initiatives, with major names taking them to Solana.

Last week, the Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation announced plans to bring tokenized versions of eligible securities to the network in the second half of 2027. The DTCC’s tokenization service launched on Oct. 28 on Canton Network and its private Besu network.

Schroders also deployed its first tokenized fund on the network in November, and Formation Securities has notified the SEC of its plan to operate a tokenized securities venue on Solana, targeting an early 2027 launch.

#4930. Boris Johnson says UK must lead blockchain development despite crypto doubts

Johnson was joined by Lord Ross Kempsell who called for regulators to work with industry participants to bring the UK to the forefront.

Former United Kingdom Prime Minister Boris Johnson has backed blockchain technology and tokenization as opportunities for the UK’s financial sector, despite persistent doubts about cryptocurrencies.

Speaking at Solana’s Breakpoint conference in London on Monday, Johnson maintained his position that Bitcoin (BTC) resembles a Ponzi scheme, following an opinion piece published in the Daily Mail in March. He questioned who investors could hold accountable if funds were stolen.

However, Johnson distinguished his criticism of digital assets from his view of the underlying technology, saying the UK should be at the forefront of tokenization.

Lord Ross Kempsell, who joined Johnson on stage, said although he shared his reservations on cryptocurrencies, “on the parts of the industry that can be brought into a regulated perimeter, which the regulators are working on, there’s a lot of scope here for a high growth industry.”

Kempsell, whom Johnson nominated for a peerage in 2022, told the panel that more than 8% of UK adults hold crypto, in line with Financial Conduct Authority-commissioned research published in December 2025. Speaking to Cointelegraph after his stage appearance, he said, “I’m a very big believer in making London and keeping London as the onchain capital for innovation.”

Related: UK financial watchdog opens consultations for DeFi guidance, crypto laws

He argued that consumer protection and financial innovation should not be treated as competing priorities, pointing to the UK’s established fintech industry as an example of how regulation can coexist with innovation in the country. He said the UK should avoid introducing a single, all-encompassing piece of crypto legislation, opting instead for “a more ad-hoc approach” to regulation.

He called for collaboration between the regulators and market participants to understand both the risks and opportunities. While he saw consumer protection as “paramount,” he felt it was unfair to single out the crypto industry as the only sector where consumer protection was at risk.

“Really what I care about is the economic growth potential that there is in this industry, the job creation potential, and also its wider links to tech, AI and traditional financial innovation. London’s always been at the center of those historically,” Kempsell told Cointelegraph.

When asked about the approach of the current administration in the UK, he highlighted that “they need to make sure that they get the fundamental market conditions right.” In his eyes, that meant making the UK a competitive player in regard to tax and facilitating business innovation.

Related: UK names 6 banks to lead first digitally native government bond

Tim Meggs, co-founder of London-based digital asset trading firm Lo:Tech, said Johnson “made a fair point about people getting burned,” but said most of the losses experienced by the industry stemmed from thin liquidity and a lack of visibility over how prices were made.

“If the FCA gets its perimeter right, the UK has a chance to make that kind of transparency the norm,” Meggs said. “But until the clearing and prime broking infrastructure catches up, good rules won’t be enough on their own to make London the place where serious digital asset trading gets done.”

Robin Nordnes, founder and CEO of Solana infrastructure firm Raiku, argued that Johnson’s question about who is in charge makes the case for blockchain, since a public ledger needs no guarantor. “Every transaction on Solana is public and can be checked by anyone, which is more than you can say for most of what happens in the City,” he said.

Nordnes warned that “if Westminster keeps treating this as retail speculation, the trading systems behind the next generation of markets will be built in New York and Singapore, and London will end up renting them.”

#4928. Bitcoin Wallet Maker Trezor Says Customer Data Was Exposed Again

Bitcoin Wallet Maker Trezor Says Customer Data Was Exposed Again

The “days since last third-party breach” counter at Trezor has reset to zero once again.

This time, hackers accessed shipping partner Skladon's systems and likely exported information tied to approximately 27,000 hardware-wallet orders, according to a company disclosure published Monday.

The intrusion occurred November 7, but Skladon did not notify Trezor until November 15. The exposed records cover orders placed between August 9 and November 7, including customers' full names, phone numbers, delivery and email addresses, plus the products they bought and their prices.

Trezor said its own systems were not compromised, and no wallet backups, PINs or passphrases were involved. The breach does not, by itself, let an attacker move a buyer's coins, but it gives them a potentially dangerous combination: a home address and evidence that someone bought a device designed to store crypto.

The latest incident follows an August shipping partner hack and a September email provider breach, making three separate third-party security incidents in a little over three months.

Trezor said some customers have already reported fraudulent phone calls. Criminals could cite the exact model and price of a wallet to make an unsolicited call, text or letter seem legitimate, or use a home address to identify someone who may hold cryptocurrency.

In August, logistics provider ShipMonk suffered a breach that initially appeared to affect fewer than 14,000 Trezor customers. An updated investigation uncovered records for another 67,000 U.S. buyers from 2019 to 2021 that ShipMonk had retained despite assurances they had been deleted, bringing the total to 80,689 people.

September brought another incident, this time at marketing email provider Brevo. Hackers used Trezor's legitimate email channel to distribute a fake security warning urging recipients to install an app that requested their wallet backups. Trezor later said 347,149 email addresses had been exported.

These weren't Trezor's first brushes with the problem. A Mailchimp insider attack targeted Trezor users in 2022, and a 2024 breach of its external support portal put the contact details of up to 66,000 people at risk.

The fallout can go beyond inbox spam. In February, fake letters were sent to hardware-wallet owners, complete with Trezor branding, holograms and QR codes leading to phishing sites.

Asked why an order from Trezor's official store involved an outside company, Trezor responded that it’s basically a logistics question, not a tech issue. “Companies of our size and global reach can’t run efficiently without third-party service providers,” the company replied to a comment on X.

Hi, companies of our size and global reach can’t run efficiently without third-party service providers. Handling as much as possible internally is the goal, but doing every part of the business ourselves isn’t realistic. Skladon is one of the partners that delivers Trezor orders,…

It said Skladon held only the information needed to deliver parcels, and handling every stage of global distribution in-house was not realistic.

Trezor says Skladon does not ship to the United States, so American buyers were not affected by this incident. Affected customers have been notified where possible, though some people who ordered between August 9 and 14 may not receive an email because Trezor had already anonymized their records.

The company has introduced anonymous pickup at parcel lockers or collection points in 18 European countries, allowing buyers to avoid supplying a home address. It is also working to shorten retention of order data from 90 to 45 days and says shipping partners will have to follow the same policy.

Trezor advises customers to ignore unexpected calls and messages about their orders, check any concerns through its official support site and never share a wallet backup. It has notified a data protection authority, but has not yet disclosed how attackers got into Skladon's systems.

#4926. Why the Clever Hans Analogy Fails Nonspeaking Spellers

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 16, 2026 | Reviewed by Abigail Fagan

Every time nonspeaking people with autism who spell to communicate make the news, someone brings up Clever Hans. You may know the story: In early 1900s Berlin, a horse seemed to do math, tapping out answers with his hoof. In 1907, the psychologist Oskar Pfungst showed what was really going on. When the questioner knew the answer and Hans could see them, Hans was right about 89% of the time; when the questioner didn't know, or Hans wore blinders, he dropped to about 6%. Questioners unknowingly leaned forward while he tapped and relaxed when he reached the right number, and Hans had learned to stop there.

The suggestion is that spellers are doing the same thing: that the words really come from the person holding the letterboard, either through subtle cues or through the communication partner's unconscious movements (the ideomotor effect). In this post, I draw on my own data and on a year of observations of nonspeaking autistic people to show why this analogy doesn't hold, and why it should be dropped for good. This is not only my personal opinion that comparing nonspeaking autistic people to a horse is cruel; it is also what the facts show.

Hans's trick worked because his task was simple: all he needed was a signal telling him when to stop tapping. Spelling is a different kind of task. To spell a sentence, a person has to choose the right letter out of 26, in the right order, again and again. A single "stop" cue, the kind that worked for Hans, can't do that. Add to this complex dance the sentences that contain words the communication partner doesn't know or normally use, or information nobody in the room expected to hear.

The best evidence here comes from studies that measured how spellers actually spell. In 2020, Vikram Jaswal and his colleagues fitted nine nonspeaking autistic letterboard users with head-mounted eye-trackers while they answered questions about an article read aloud to them, with an assistant holding the board.¹ They pointed to about one letter per second, and 94% of the letters they pointed to were correct. On average, they looked at 71% of the correct letters before pointing to them, about half a second ahead. Like fluent non-autistic typists, they paused longer before the first letter of a new word and were faster on common letter pairs. As the authors point out, cueing would require a separate signal for every letter, picked from 26 options, which would be slow and error-prone. What they found was fast, accurate, and planned.

Some critics argue that the partner moves the board slightly toward the right letter. A 2024 study from the same lab removed the partner entirely.² Thirty-one nonspeaking autistic teenagers and adults played a game on an iPad mounted on a stand, tapping letters as they lit up. No one held the iPad. As a group, they tapped faster when the letters spelled a sentence they had just heard than when similar letters or symbols came in a meaningless order. They were also faster on common letter pairs, and they paused before the first letter of each new word. These are signs of knowing how to spell.

A third study had participants spell on a virtual letterboard in an augmented-reality headset, with no one holding it; they averaged 95.8% accuracy on dictated words.³

If spellers were reading their partners the way Hans read his questioners, blocking their view of the partner should stop them, just as the blinders stopped Hans. As it happens, we've been testing this with a few spellers by placing a rigid panel, about five feet tall and three feet wide, between the speller and the communication partner, so that the spellers cannot see the partners' faces or bodies. The results? They keep spelling fluently and without hesitation. After the very first word, one of them even spelled: "Better with the barrier."

The second problem with the horse analogy is the content. Hans only ever answered questions, and only with answers the questioner already had in mind. Spellers start conversations, ask for things, and say things that surprise the people around them.

A young man I visited this year began spelling at fifteen and spells mainly with his mother. After I spent a full day visiting him at school, he was able that evening to have a conversation about his day and to spell the names of two kids who had visited the classroom; neither his mother nor I knew their names, and his mother hadn't been at school. He was also able to express his feelings towards his brothers and his wish for more independence: "I'm seventeen, let me go by myself," he spelled when he asked to walk to the bus stop alone. Among his other wishes, he asked to go into the ocean with his brothers, wearing a life jacket, and told his parents he wanted a North Face backpack, not the Under Armour one they had picked.

When his mother first began spelling with him on her own, one of his first requests was to see a Broadway show, which she had never imagined he wanted. He also spelled things his partner would not choose to hear. In front of me, he told his mother that a book I'd brought was boring, and she was embarrassed. On another occasion, when he came downstairs while his mother was out, he spelled to his father: "Dad, stop giving me chicken nuggets. I just want to spend time together."

But perhaps his most unexpected request came when he spelled out that he wanted to go to Disney World for his sixteenth birthday, something the family would never have expected, given his sensitivity to crowded and noisy places. They went; he wore noise-cancelling headphones, and he had a blast. "I tolerated it because I wanted to be there," he told me, and made fun of how his father threw up on rides.

A cueing account says the words come from his mother's expectations. However, these messages went against what his family expected, and they changed how the family lives with him. This is exactly what we should be looking for when we ask whether a speller is the author of their words: requests, opinions, and jokes that the partner did not anticipate and would not have chosen. The Hans model has no way to produce them.

Comparing a person to a horse responding to signals is not a neutral choice. It fits an old and outdated assumption: that people who don't speak don't understand. For decades, nonspeaking autistic people were labeled profoundly intellectually disabled, often without any assessment that accommodated their motor differences. The young man I visited spent his early school years in a program built around life skills and weather charts, and he calls his current high school classroom what it is: segregated. When a speller's words are dismissed with a one-line reference to Clever Hans, the speller pays the price: in which school they attend, whether their requests are taken seriously, and whether anyone asks for their opinion at all.

And, if you are still not convinced after reading this, perhaps hearing from the nonspeakers themselves will help. Here’s what they have been expressing about the effects of spelling on their lives:

"It was a game changer for me. It provided me with freedom I dreamed about for 15 years. Now, instead of being an observer, I am an active participant."

"Learning to spell changed my life trajectory. Before, I was treated like a toddler, and my world collapsed in on me… doctors, experts, teachers got it all wrong.… So communication is not just a basic need but a human right."

1. Jaswal, V. K., Wayne, A., & Golino, H. (2020). Eye-tracking reveals agency in assisted autistic communication. Scientific Reports, 10, 7882.

2. Jaswal, V. K., Lampi, A. J., & Stockwell, K. M. (2024). Literacy in nonspeaking autistic people. Autism, 28(10), 2503–2514.

3. Alabood, L., Dow, T., Feeley, K. B., Jaswal, V. K., & Krishnamurthy, D. (2024). From letterboards to holograms: Advancing assistive technology for nonspeaking autistic individuals with the HoloBoard. In Proceedings of the CHI Conference on Human Factors in Computing Systems (CHI '24). ACM. https://doi.org/10.1145/3613904.3642626

#4925. Bitmine edges toward 5% of ETH supply as treasuries report buys amid market slump

Strategy, Strive, Bitmine and Cypherpunk disclosed fresh additions to their crypto treasuries, spanning Bitcoin, Ether and Zcash.

Publicly traded crypto treasury companies Bitmine, Strive and Cypherpunk Technologies disclosed increases in their cryptocurrency holdings on Monday, joining Strategy in accumulating digital assets despite a broader market downturn.

Bitmine, the largest publicly traded Ether treasury company, said it added 7,653 Ether (ETH) over the past week, bringing its total holdings to 6.08 million ETH, about 4.97% of its total supply and bringing it ever closer to its stated aim of accumulating 5% of the ETH supply, according to a Monday announcement.

Top Bitcoin treasury companies Strategy and Strive also reported purchases. Strategy acquired 485 Bitcoin (BTC) for $33.4 million, while Strive bought 251 BTC for $17.5 million, bringing its holdings to 43,404 BTC — just 110 BTC behind Twenty One Capital, the third-largest publicly traded Bitcoin holder, according to BitcoinTreasuries.net data.

Zcash (ZEC) treasury and mining company Cypherpunk Technologies reported buying 3,775.72 ZEC for $3.48 million since September and mining another 5,721.35 ZEC in October. The additions brought its holdings to 341,858.75 ZEC, or around 2% of circulating supply.

The purchases come amid a broader cryptocurrency sell-off. Bitcoin was trading around $65,017, down 12.9% over the past seven days and 20% over the past month. ETH fell 14.2% and ZEC dropped 22.9% over the past week, according to CoinGecko data as of 8:08 pm UTC on Monday.

Bitcoin is down around 20% over the past month. Source: CoinGecko

#4924. Alibaba's Qwen 4 Max Leads China's AI Agent Race—But You Can't Download It

Alibaba's Qwen 4 Max Leads China's AI Agent Race—But You Can't Download It

Alibaba released Qwen 4 Max on Monday, its most powerful AI model yet and the first flagship built on its new Qwen 4 architecture.

It is also the highest-ranked Chinese model on Arena's agent leaderboard, putting the company ahead of Tencent, Moonshot AI and DeepSeek in that particular test.

The new model has 2.4 trillion parameters, can process text, images and video, and accepts up to one million tokens of context. Parameters are all the different dials a model can handle and tweak when processing information. Tokens are basically the smallest unit of information a model can handle. Its current configuration is enough room for lengthy codebases or stacks of documents.

Alibaba uses a mixture-of-experts design, which keeps most of those parameters idle for any given word it processes. Qwen 4 Max activates 71 billion at a time, compared with 95 billion for Qwen 3.8 Max, allowing a very large model to use less computing power per token.

On Agent Arena, Qwen 4 Max placed 14th among individual models and made Alibaba the fifth-ranked laboratory overall.

The leaderboard evaluates real agent sessions using task completion, user satisfaction and how well models respond to corrections, then scores each model by its net percentage improvement over an average competitor. Qwen 4 Max posted a preliminary 7.46% score across 14,727 sessions, the highest among Chinese entries.

Its visual work may be the more interesting part for regular users. The Code Arena web-development ranking compares websites built by AI models using head-to-head user votes and a points-based rating system. Qwen 4 Max debuted sixth globally at 1,750 points, up 74 from its predecessor, and placed eighth in categories for branding and reference-based design.

That puts it in the same neighborhood as the best American models at turning a request, a screenshot or a rough idea into a working interface that actually looks good.

Alibaba's demos go further, showing the model building game environments and translating factory layouts into 3D models, although those are company-selected examples rather than independent tests.

The visual capability is for understanding images and video and producing code or other text-based outputs. It is not the same as releasing a new image generator.

Qwen 4 Max costs $1.50 per million input tokens and $4.50 per million generated tokens through QwenCloud, a 25% reduction from the previous flagship's $2 and $6 rates. Reused input served from an automatic cache costs $0.075 per million tokens, which can make repetitive workflows substantially cheaper.

Alibaba says its internal testing across hundreds of professional specialties found that the model used 35% fewer tokens and reduced average task costs by roughly 60% compared with Qwen 3.8 Max. In other words, it should be cheaper to execute tasks on this model even against competitors with a similar price..

The bigger limitation for the community is that Qwen 4 Max is closed-weight. Developers can use it through an API or Alibaba's apps, but they cannot download its internal parameters, modify it or run it privately on their own hardware.

Qwen has become a foundation for the open-model ecosystem. An August report from Hugging Face counted 151,448 Qwen-based derivative models on its platform, about 4.7 times the number built specifically on Meta's Llama family. Those include community fine-tunes and optimized versions that power local assistants and specialized tools.

Alibaba opened the weights of Qwen 3.8 Max earlier this year, despite having kept some previous Max models proprietary. It also previewed elements of the Qwen 4 architecture with smaller models months before Monday's release.

The launch lands in a crowded Chinese race. Moonshot's Kimi K3 pushed enormous open-weight models toward Western performance, while DeepSeek has been competing hard on API prices.

Qwen 4 Max leads some Chinese rankings, but American labs still hold the top spots in Arena's overall agent and web-development tables. They are also considerably more expensive.

Alibaba says smaller Qwen 4 models will be released with open weights, giving developers a chance to build on this generation rather than simply rent its biggest model. It has not announced their sizes or release dates.

#4922. Solana Foundation Backs CoinGecko as Crypto Data Giant Eyes Trading

Solana Foundation Backs CoinGecko as Crypto Data Giant Eyes Trading

CoinGecko, one of crypto’s most widely used price-tracking sites, wants to do more than show users where markets are heading.

The crypto data platform said Tuesday that it has received backing from the Solana Foundation, the Switzerland-based nonprofit that supports the Solana network, to speed its expansion into market intelligence, new digital asset products and, potentially, trading.

The announcement came on the final day of Solana’s Breakpoint conference in London.

The Solana Foundation did not specify the size of the commitment, but a representative for the foundation told Decrypt it is taking a majority stake in CoinGecko. The terms were not disclosed.

CoinGecko said it will focus on three areas: expanding data coverage and analytics across blockchain ecosystems, exploring trading features that let users act onchain, and building simpler experiences for users worldwide.

“We’ve helped millions of people discover and understand this industry, but the opportunity ahead is much bigger,” said Bobby Ong, CoinGecko’s CEO and co-founder.

Vibhu Norby, chief product officer at the Solana Foundation, said the foundation believes the “next wave of adoption will come from making crypto dramatically easier to use.” (Disclosure: Decrypt’s parent company, Dastan, is building products on Solana, and Decrypt uses CoinGecko market data.)

Founded in 2014, CoinGecko says it provides data on more than 50 million crypto assets across more than 2,000 exchanges.

CoinGecko said it will keep serving users with the same commitment to “reliable, comprehensive market coverage and transparent methodologies.” Co-founder and President TM Lee said its commitment to accurate, reliable data “remains unchanged.”

The deal is one of several Solana announcements timed around Breakpoint. DTCC said last week that it plans to connect its tokenization service to Solana, with tokenized stocks, ETFs and Treasuries expected on the network in the second half of 2027.

At a Breakpoint panel, executives from DTCC and Barclays said tokenization’s biggest near-term payoff lies in putting idle collateral to work.

Editor's note: This article was updated after publication to include comment from the Solana Foundation.
